# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.
The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/).

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **The relay and air-time model now includes the state changes' TIME repeats, MOVE and the sweep** (decisions RL-1 to RL-8; `simulator/atalaia_sim/relay_study.py` `Engine(state_changes=...)` and `Policy.time_repeat_gaps_ms`, new `simulator/atalaia_sim/state_load.py`, `simulator/tests/test_state_load.py`, `docs/review/state-load-results.md`, `docs/runbooks/radio-field-test.md`): since RB-8 a start, pause or resume is CMD + TIME and four more TIMEs, and each box relays each one; the study never had them. For a 20-box field on the default 10 % sub-band a state change costs the controller 0.47 s of air (0.33 s of it the repeats) and a box 0.04 s; a typical hour (7 changes) adds 2.2 s to the controller (+0.06 point of the hour) and 0.31 s to a box (+0.01 point), nothing refused; every limit and margin the documents state still holds, so the controller is unchanged. On a 1 % sub-band the controller's margin goes from 0.11 to 0.04 point (past the limit with about ten pauses an hour; the modem's guard refuses the extra frame), and a sweep once a minute can spend a small sub-band: both written up with the mitigations considered. The 20-box field test plan measures the repeats, the start latency and a channel change plus sweep.
- **The console says what a change of channel does to the free boxes** (decision RB-13, RFC 0001 Amendment 19; `controller/console/src/components/radio-settings.js`, `box-invites.js`, `app.js`; `atalaia_controller/reinvite.py` and `engine.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_boxes.dart` and `controller_channel.dart`): the radio settings and the `radio` stream message carry `move` (`telling` with the MOVEs said so far of three, then `moved`, null before a change), on both controllers and in a shared contract case (`S7c MOVE progress`). The channel panel shows *Telling the free boxes on the old channel ... said n of 3 times* while the MOVEs repeat (about 5 s) and then *The controller has moved to ...*; *Look for boxes* (a button in the panel between games, and the invite dialog's) shows which earlier channels were swept (*Also looked on the earlier channels: 869.525 MHz*), how many boxes answered since, and a help line: a box still missing needs to be adopted again or be switched on, free and near during the next change. Four languages; the shell's file list is unchanged, so the service worker's cache name stays.
- **Indoor beacon positions on iPhone** (decisions IOSB-1 to IOSB-8; `app/ios/Runner/BeaconScanPlugin.swift`, `AppDelegate.swift`, `LocationPlugin.swift`, `app/lib/player/player_screens.dart`): a Swift plugin on the same channels and message shapes as Android's `BeaconScanPlugin.kt` (`org.atalaia.app/beacons` and `/sightings`), over CoreLocation: one `startRangingBeacons(satisfying:)` constraint per registered UUID (a Field Box's per-field UUID and the tags' alike), the one-second readings grouped into the game's beacon window, one average signal strength per beacon, the same -95 dBm floor and strongest-beacon rule in the shared Dart code. It needs only the location *When in use* (no Bluetooth permission, no *Always*; the explain-first dialog and *Continue* / *Not now* are as on Android, with iPhone texts in pt, en, de and zh-Hant, and the location usage text names the beacons) and ranges only while the app is on the screen: a locked phone falls back to GPS and the last floor, which the player screen says. Dart tests with a fake channel cover the iOS path; **the Swift has not been compiled or run** (no Mac here: CI's `ios-build` and the maintainer's iPhone are the first check; checklist in `docs/runbooks/ios-beacons-iphone.md`).

- **A standby controller serves the same box-beacon registry after a failover** (decision BBC-20, RFC 0001 Amendment 21; `simulator/atalaia_sim/join.py`, `app/lib/protocol/join_payload.dart`, `controller/atalaia_controller/box_beacons.py` and `engine.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_box_beacons.dart` and `controller_engine.dart`, `protocol/vectors/join.json` `box_beacons`, `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`): the GM's box-beacon choices (the field switch, the boxes disabled, the rooms given) were settings of the primary only, so after a failover the registry served to phones and the join's key 19 could differ from what the GM had set. The standby's join is what goes from a primary to its standby, so it now carries them: the standby reads key 19, and a new optional join key 21, `box_beacons` (a standby's payload only, at most 200 bytes), holds the disabled nodes and the rooms. A join made again for the game and key a standby already follows is a refresh of those settings and the box language instead of a conflict (so later choices can reach it). Tests: a failover with the choices changed on the primary in both controllers (`controller/tests/test_join.py`, `app/test/controller/controller_box_beacons_test.dart`), the codecs' `box_beacons` vectors (18 refused, 3 round-tripped) and the shared contract case "S7 failover: a standby takes the primary's box-beacon choices". A box's place on the field view is still each controller's own.

### Changed

- **Bomb / Rush: an explosion ends the game, the arming team winning** (decision GE-10; `modes/bomb/node.lua`, `mode.yaml` 0.3.0, `modes/bomb/tests`, `modes/README.md`, the GM and player manuals en+pt, the site's modes page): right after `bomb.exploded` and the exploded screen the script calls `game.win` for the team that armed the bomb, so the box shows EXPLODED and the controller ends the game (GE-1 to GE-6). A defuse does not end the game (Bomb's rule is unchanged); Bomb has no time limit; after a power loss in a controller-led game the box starts afresh, so no explosion; Sabotage is unchanged. Bomb with Codes loads at 54.1 KB of the Reduced box's 56 KiB test limit. New test `reboot_armed`.
- **A phone that is already sharing picks up registry changes during the game** (decision BLE-20, amending BLE-5; `controller/atalaia_controller/api.py` `BEACONS_CHANGED` and `make_route`, the app's `controller_stream.dart` and `controller_field_beacons.dart`, `app/lib/player/field_feed.dart`, `player_screens.dart`, `position/beacon_scanner.dart`, `position_reporter.dart`; vectors `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`): a Field Box placed, moved to another level or disabled, or a tag added or deleted after a player began to share was never used by phones already in the game (BBC-8). The stream message `beacons`, which only staff got, now goes to every connection with no data; a phone that is sharing reads `GET /api/v1/beacons` again two seconds after the last one (several changes, one read; also after a reconnect's hello) and restarts its scan through the same start call only when the set of UUIDs changed, so a change of room name alone costs no radio work. A field whose first beacon appears during the game asks the player once, as at the start (*Continue* / *Not now*). No polling and no new permission; the controller (Python and Dart) and the app change, the Android and iOS plugins do not.
- **The Field Box's internal RAM budget: Wi-Fi at a join and the iBeacon fit together, never at the same time** (decisions BBC-13 to BBC-18; `firmware/esp32/sdkconfig.defaults`, `main/psram_attr.h`, `lvgl_conf/lv_conf.h`, `main/box_beacon_esp.c`, `tools/check_fw_ram.py`): found on the bench on 2026-10-29, when the box's internal RAM was gone at a game's join (Wi-Fi `ESP_ERR_NO_MEM`, then `BLE_INIT: Malloc failed` and an interrupt watchdog reset). Static internal RAM left for the heap went from 45,704 to 194,049 bytes: LVGL's 48 KB widget heap and the game's big static buffers (the preset, the box, the runner, the built-in presets' scratch, the stored-preset lists, the screen template: about 62 KB) live in PSRAM now (`ATALAIA_PSRAM_BSS`, `CONFIG_SPIRAM_ALLOW_BSS_SEG_EXTERNAL_MEMORY`; off in the Wokwi, modem and pod images), and the Bluetooth controller is sized for one advertiser. The beacon starts only with 40 KB of internal RAM free and a 12 KB block, cleans up a controller that failed half-way, retries three times and then stays off for that game, never a panic; the Wi-Fi start waits for the controller to be down; the display falls back to one draw buffer or fewer lines. A log line with the internal and DMA figures at boot, at the Wi-Fi start and around the beacon's start (`mem_monitor_log`), and CI's `esp32-build` fails when the image leaves under 120 KiB of internal RAM (`tools/check_fw_ram.py`). The beacon stays on in the Field Box image. **Not yet run on the board**: `docs/runbooks/box-beacon-bench.md` has the checklist and the figures to expect.
- **The boot splash is a build asset** (decision LOGO-6; `firmware/esp32/assets/splash.pgm`, `main/splash_logo.cmake`, `CONFIG_ATALAIA_SPLASH_LOGO`): the Atalaia tower as a plain-text 30 x 60 PGM that the build embeds, so a builder replaces one file (or points the Kconfig option at theirs) to build a box with another splash; the project's release images always carry the tower. Steps in the firmware guide (section 4, en and pt) and `firmware/esp32/README.md`.

- **A release build refuses a splash that is not the Atalaia tower** (decision LOGO-7; `tools/release_package.py`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `controller/tests/test_release_package.py`): `package` fails when `assets/splash.pgm` differs from the pinned SHA-256, the build's `sdkconfig` has another `CONFIG_ATALAIA_SPLASH_LOGO`, or the generated `splash_logo.c` is not that file's pixels; documented in CONTRIBUTING.md (Cutting a release) and `firmware/esp32/README.md`.

### Removed

- **The Field Box no longer fetches or shows the field's logo** (decision LOGO-5, superseding PKG-1 and LOGO-1 to LOGO-4; maintainer's call, 2026-10-29): a join starts no Wi-Fi for a logo (the bench's failing fetch), the box keeps no `logo.bin`, and draws only the Atalaia tower. Gone: the firmware's `box_logo` and its tests and four screen goldens, the controllers' `GET /api/v1/field/box-logo` and `/box-logo/release` and `PUT /api/v1/field/branding/logo/box` (Python and Dart), the console's JPEG and WebP conversion for boxes (`box-logo.js`, its texts, its browser tests), `tools/box_logo_vectors.py` and `protocol/vectors/box_logo.json`. The field's logo stays in the app, the console, the scoreboard, the poster and the site server's sync. The join and the radio protocol never carried a logo key, so nothing on the wire is retired; `protocol/mode-packages.md` section 8 is kept as a retirement note.
- **A box that kept the old field logo removes it at boot** (decision LOGO-7; `firmware/esp32/main/legacy_files.c`): a box that fetched a logo before LOGO-5 still holds `logo.bin` (and maybe a half-written `logo.new`) in its LittleFS storage, which nothing reads now. The first boot of this firmware does one `stat` per name and an `unlink` for a file that exists, logging `removed N old field-logo file(s) from storage` once; a clean box pays nothing. Those two names were the only files the logo feature wrote. Host test `test_legacy_files.c`.

- **Site server: the daily retention purge deletes anonymised accounts nothing refers to any more** (`personal_data.py`, `purge`, new count `anonymised_users`): a `deleted-...` row (what erasure leaves; also what an account merge will leave) was kept forever. It now goes once no audit entry and no row of any table with a foreign key to `users.id` refers to it (the check is built from the models' foreign keys, so a later table is covered); the audit entries' own 36 months free it in the same pass. Hosted mode: per field, under the field's role. The privacy notice drafts (pt, en), README and DEPLOY say how long the remains of an erased account are kept.
- **A box's `game.end` now ends the game on the controller when the mode has no `field.lua`** (decision GE-1; `controller/atalaia_controller/engine.py`, the app's `controller_engine.dart`, the simulator's `sim.py`): in Sabotage (the time limit) and any mode whose `node.lua` calls `game.win` / `game.finish`, the box emitted `game.end` and the controller only logged it, so the game stayed running (found on the bench). The **first** `game.end` from a box assigned in the live game, while it runs and this controller leads it, ends the game with the event's winner (one of the game's teams) or as a draw, at the next tick; later ones are only logged. In a mode with a `field.lua` nothing changes: the script decides. The controller's own `game.end` carries who ended it (`by_node`, `by_name`; new event data fields 40 and 41) and the console's log says *ended by box ...* in four languages. A `game.end` that arrives while the game is paused does not end it. Shared contract cases in `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json` (three) run on both controllers.

### Fixed
- **A flaky remote-GM test** (decision FL-20; `controller/tests/test_remote_gm.py`): `test_they_travel_on_the_stream_when_changed_and_stop_with_the_game` read `positions_sent` the instant the fake site had the `positions` frame, but the stream thread bumps the counter after the frame is on the wire, so under CPU load it saw 0 (`0 != 1`). It now waits for the counter. Test only; the product was right.

- **The Field Box left an update's refusal or failure on its screen for hours** (`firmware/esp32/main/status_hold.c`, `shell.c`; found on the bench on 2026-10-29: an idle box refused `@update 9.9.9 deadbeef` and kept "ATUALIZAÇÃO | bad_offer" up until the maintainer looked): the status screens of the update and of the mode fetch (a refusal, a failure, a fetch's result; also the refusal while a game is played) are shown for 8 s and then the screen of the box's state comes back (the picker, the lobby, the last game's final score, the running game's screen: the last one drawn for the state). A download or fetch still running keeps its progress screen; a screen drawn over the status meanwhile cancels the return; a successful install still restarts. Host test: `test_status_hold.c`.
- **The Field Box ran out of internal RAM at a game's join and reset** (`firmware/esp32/sdkconfig.defaults`; found on the bench on 2026-10-29): with Bluetooth compiled in for the box beacon (about 25 KB of internal RAM), the Wi-Fi for the field's logo could not start (`ESP_ERR_NO_MEM`), the Bluetooth controller's allocation then failed and the watchdog reset the box. Bluetooth and the box beacon are **off** in the Field Box build until the beacon fits beside the Wi-Fi (decision BBC-13); the beacon code, the controller's box registry and the phones' scanning stay, and GM tags work as before. The Wi-Fi logo fetch was already short of memory before (`esp-aes: Failed to allocate memory` on the morning's bench); with Bluetooth off it fails cleanly (`no_wifi`, the box keeps the Atalaia logo) and the game starts.
- **A change of the field's channel no longer strands the free boxes** (RFC 0001 Amendment 19; `core/src/field_frame.c`, `firmware/esp32/main/box_lobby.c`, `controller/atalaia_controller/engine.py` and `reinvite.py`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/lobby.py`, the phone's `controller_channel.dart` and `controller_boxes.dart`): found on the bench on 2026-10-29, when a radio scan moved the controller's field channel (869.525 to 869.925 MHz) and the Field Box, idle between games on its last game's channel, heard neither DISCOVER nor invites until the controller was set back. The controller now says where the field went in a new field frame, **MOVE** (kind 5: the new channel and a revision that only rises, sealed with the field key), three times on the old channel before it tunes its modem to the new one, then asks the boxes there with a DISCOVER; a free box follows, keeps the channel and the revision in NVS and announces itself. A box that was away still listens on an older channel: the controller remembers the field's last four channels and *Find boxes* (`POST /api/v1/boxes/discover`) now also says the MOVE on each of them (`"sweep": [...]` in the answer, at most once a minute). A free box also keeps its idle channel across a reboot (it used to fall back to the region's default). A box without this firmware needs a card once, as before. Vectors in `protocol/vectors/field_frame.json`, C, Python and Dart codecs, host tests (`test_lobby_move`, `simulator/tests/test_move_sim.py`, `controller/tests/test_reinvite.py` `MoveTests`, the phone's `controller_channel_test.dart`).
- **A started, paused or resumed game reaches the boxes in about a second, not up to 30 s** (RFC 0001 Amendment 19, part 2; `simulator/atalaia_sim/field_controller.py`, `app/lib/controller/field_controller.dart`, `firmware/esp32/main/box_game.c`): found on the bench on 2026-10-29, when the joined box logged "game started" 21 s after the GM pressed Start. A start went out as one CMD and one TIME, each once, and the CMD has no acknowledgement and no resend; when both were lost the box learned of the start from the next 30 s beacon. After every start, pause and resume the controller now repeats the state as a TIME at +1, +2.5, +5 and +10 s (about 0.36 s of air; a new state change replaces the repeats due, an end is not repeated: GAME_OVER does that), and a box's tick starts or pauses a game its link already knows is running or paused, which a START heard while the box could not act on it (still fetching its modes) used to leave stuck. `controller/tests/test_box_e2e.py` drops the first CMD and TIME through the real box firmware and pins start, pause and resume under two seconds; the shared "the radio" case in `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json` pins the repeat times for both controllers.
- **Preview: the live demo's `demo-init` failed on every update after the first** (`server/docker-compose.preview.yml`): it changes `/secrets`' mode, which once the folder belongs to the demo user (10001) needs the FOWNER capability it did not have. Found on the preview server on 2026-10-29.
- **Site server: the "consent required" error of a social login shows at the box, and says where the box is** (`server/atalaia_server/web/static/staff/login.js`, `staff.css`, `templates/staff/login.html`, `staff_i18n.py`, and the app's four strings): refusing a new account without the agreement was right, but the message was a thin note at the top while the checkbox is below the provider buttons and said "above". It is now an error box with `role="alert"` just above the checkbox, the checkbox highlighted and focused (`aria-invalid`), cleared when it is ticked; the text in pt, en, de and zh-tw says the box is below the buttons and to choose the provider again.
- **Site server: the social login settings never reached the server's container** (`server/docker-compose.yml`): the `ATALAIA_OIDC_*` settings DEPLOY.md tells the operator to put in `.env` were not in the server's environment, so Google, Microsoft and Apple stayed off in every Docker deployment (found on the preview server on 2026-10-29). A test now checks the compose file lists them.

- **App: an Android prop's box penalty never reached the controller** (BSC-1, STATE key 9): the prop's STATE carried the net score as its counters, which the controller merges by maximum, so a penalty could not lower the total, and neither its Wi-Fi link (`controller_props.dart`) nor its radio link (`pod_radio_link.dart`) carried `lost`. The prop now sends the gains as counters and `lost` (only when nonzero, as a box does) on both links; a node snapshot carries `gained` and `lost`.
- **App: the field screen kept the login's scroll position** after logging in (each step of the site screen now starts at its top).

### Added

- **The live demo also shows a multi-story building** (decisions DEMO-10 to DEMO-18; `server/preview/demo/field/building.py`, `director.py`, `proxy.py`, `player/`, `home/`): the director alternates the open-field game with a game in an invented **three-storey building**: levels "R/C", "1.º andar" and "2.º andar", a floor plan each drawn by the demo's own code, a georeference of its own, fifteen registered BLE beacons, the three boxes on three levels, and twelve simulated players walking between rooms and floors, six located by beacon (`ble` reports, as the app sends them), five by GPS with a floor picked (`level` in the report) and one "Not sure" (`level` null, drawn hollow by the player's map). The player's view gets floor buttons, a plan per level (`/player/field.png?level=N`, a single digit or the field image), the beacons and the player's room; the home page (pt, en, de, zh-tw) says the scenario exists and how to switch floors in the console and in the player's view. The proxy's allow-list already covered the levels and beacons endpoints and needed nothing new (the read token still gets only each beacon's id and uuid); the player's view reads the demo's own invented registry from a file the director leaves for it. Tests: `preview-demo-unit` (the plans, georeference, registry against the controller's own checks, routes, the three ways of being located, the level and beacon paths of the proxy) and `preview-demo` (a building game is played in turn: levels, plans, beacons, boxes on three levels, positions on several levels by beacon, GPS and not sure, and the open field comes back with levels off).
- **Site server: a social sign-up records the privacy-notice version the person actually saw** (decision CONS-7; `api/login_methods.py`, `models.py`, migration `0025_oidc_notice_version`, `web/static/staff/login.js`, `staff_i18n.py`; tests in `tests/test_login_methods.py`, `tests/test_migrations.py`). `POST /api/v1/auth/oidc/{provider}/start` takes an optional `notice_version`: with a ticked consent and a version that is not the current one it answers 409 `notice_version_changed` (with `current_version`); otherwise the version is kept on the login row, and the callback checks it again before making the account (`oidc_error=notice_version_changed` if the notice changed meanwhile) and records the consent for that version, instead of whatever the server's version is at callback time. Clients that send nothing behave as before. The staff login page names the version it read from `/api/v1/privacy-notice` and, on the 409 or the error, unticks the box and explains, in pt, en, de and zh-tw.
- **The Field Boxes advertise as iBeacons, so a box doubles as a floor and room locator** (the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-29; decisions BBC-1 to BBC-12; RFC 0001 **Amendment 20**; `protocol/box-beacon.md`, `docs/runbooks/box-beacon-bench.md`). A Field Box joined to a game advertises a non-connectable iBeacon (NimBLE as a broadcaster only, **every 500 ms at 0 dBm**, from the join to the end of the game, taken down again with its memory when it ends, never between games and never while the Wi-Fi is in use). The **UUID is the field's**, `HMAC-SHA256(join key, "atalaia box beacon uuid v1")[:16]`, so a phone filters by one UUID and nothing travels; the **major is the constant 45253** (a GM's tag may not use it, so ids never collide) and the **minor is the box's node id**. The controllers (Pi `box_beacons.py`, Android `controller_box_beacons.dart`) **register each box's beacon by themselves from its place on the field view** (level, x, y; the room is the box's name or one the GM gives), so moving a box or changing its level moves its beacon; the console's beacons panel lists them as **Field Box** (not editable as a tag; disable one, give it a room) and the registry served to players gains the box UUID and nothing else; a phone that hears a box is located exactly as with a tag, and the app and its Android plugin needed no change (a unit test takes a box's real advertisement bytes through the plugin's filter and parse and the scanner). **The GM's switch** (`PUT /api/v1/box-beacons`, console checkbox) rides in the join payload's new optional **key 19** (`false` only; left out when on, so every older payload keeps its bytes; the three codecs and `protocol/vectors/join.json`'s `box_beacon` section). Kconfig `ATALAIA_BOX_BEACON` (default on for the Field Box; the Wokwi build and the modem build without Bluetooth, the pod keeps its own). Field Box image: 1,974,080 to 2,135,712 bytes (+8.2 %, 49 % of the slot still free); static internal RAM +25 KB. Tests: firmware host tests (the bytes against `protocol/vectors/box_beacon.json`, which `tools/box_beacon_vectors.py` writes from `hashlib` alone; the phases and key 19 in `test_box_game.c`), controller tests (`test_box_beacons.py`, the shared contract case "box beacons" on both controllers), Dart and console browser tests. **Not run on a board or a phone yet**: the runbook is the bench checklist (the Bluetooth controller's memory beside the Wi-Fi's, the interval against an Android scan, the LoRa link with the beacon on and off).
- **Site server: a social account chooses its username, and any account renames itself with its own login method; two accounts of one person can be merged** (decisions LG-10 to LG-12; `api/account_merge.py`, `api/login_methods.py`, `oidc.py`, `tokens.py`, migration `0024_oidc_name_purpose`, `/staff/account`, four languages; the maintainer's tests of 2026-10-29 on the preview server). (1) A new account made by a social login is `user-` and hex, and could not rename itself (the rename asked for a password it does not have): the browser now goes to the Account page's **"Choose your username"** step right after the account is made (`new_account` in the `complete` answer; empty field, the usual rules, skippable), and the page's username section renames any account at any time, confirmed with the account's own method (the password, or a login from the last ten minutes: a passkey, a linked provider, a TOTP code). (2) For the provider the account signed up with (Google, Microsoft; not Apple) a **"Use my Google name" / "Use my Microsoft name"** button runs a round with the extra scope `profile` only when pressed (`purpose: name`) and pre-fills a username derived from the name in the address fragment; **the name is never stored** (not in `users`, not in the audit log) and normal logins still ask for `openid` only. DEPLOY.md: Google's consent screen must list the non-sensitive `profile` scope; Microsoft needs nothing. The privacy notice drafts (pt, en) and the sign-up consent text (four languages) say the provider's name is read only when the person asks and is not kept. (3) **Merging accounts** (Account page): linking a provider login that belongs to another account (a second social login that made its own account) now offers to merge that account into this one, confirmed as any sensitive change; logins, passkeys, roles, registrations, consents, club memberships, player ids and the rest move, duplicates keep this account's, the other account is anonymised and its sessions end, the other account's password is never carried over, a B with a staff role (server administrator, admin, gm, marshal) is refused, and both accounts get an audit entry. Signing in with the other login while this one is open is just a sign-in to the other. Tests: `tests/test_account_names_merge.py` (30), `tests/test_account_merge_hosted.py` (a merge as a field's own database role, another field untouched).
- **`@lobby` on the box's serial console also prints the field's MOVE revision and how many MOVEs the box took and refused**, and `docs/runbooks/channel-move-and-start-latency-bench.md` is the coordinator's checklist for both fixes above on the two boards (a channel change with a box idle, a start with the first frames lost, how to tell the cases apart in the serial logs).
- **App: the site server's privacy notice and consent again** (CONS-1 to CONS-6; `app/lib/site/privacy_notice.dart`, `privacy_screens.dart`): the signed-in field screen shows a *Read and confirm* prompt when the account's consent is outdated or missing, the notice in the app's language (pt-PT, en, de, zh-tw; the server's fallback is said), a tick and *Confirm* that records the version on the screen (a notice that changed meanwhile is read again). Nothing is blocked (CONS-4). The login has a *Read the privacy notice* button and the notice stays one tap away on the field screen. Manuals updated.
- **App: the social sign-up carries the notice version** (the OIDC start's `notice_version`): the app sends the version it showed with the ticked consent; a server that does not take the field yet refuses it (422) and the app asks again without it. The server side is still to do (see the report: `StartIn.notice_version`, a 409 `notice_version_changed`).
- **App: the social sign-up's retry without `notice_version` is kept, but only for an older server's own refusal** (decision APF-10; `app/lib/site/site_auth.dart`, `site_client.dart`, `test/site/privacy_hub_test.dart`). The server now takes the field (CONS-7), but the app also talks to older self-hosted servers and the site has no version or capabilities endpoint to ask. The start is asked again without the field only for a 422 that names `notice_version` as `extra_forbidden`; every other 422 surfaces. Tests: a new server (one request, version sent), an old server (retry), a real validation 422 (no retry, error shown).
- **App: the chrono link between a hosted hub's fields** (SVX-2; `app/lib/site/hub_share.dart`): the state, a one-time code, entering another field's code, unlinking; the button and screen only exist in hosted mode (404 `not_hosted` hides them).
- **Bench builds: `@hold <button 1-4> <ms>` on the box's serial console holds a team button** (`firmware/esp32/main/shell.c`, only with `CONFIG_ATALAIA_SERIAL_KEYS`, i.e. `sdkconfig.bench` and the Wokwi build): the projected-score runbook and the penalty check ran on the bench on 2026-10-29 without hands on the board; results in `docs/runbooks/projected-score-bench.md`.

## [0.5.0] - 2026-10-29

A **pre-alpha, not field-tested** pre-release. **USB flash required once:** the Field Box (and the Satellite), the modem and the I/O pod get a new partition table with 4 MB app slots, which an update over the air cannot change; flash this release over USB once, **without** erasing, and the box keeps its adoption, keys and settings (firmware guide, section 3.3). Also new: the Sabotage game mode, box penalties and a projected score, I/O pod settings over the radio, data retention per field, the published privacy notice with consent asked again, chrono sharing across a hosted hub's fields, a database role per field in hosted mode, and a read-only live demo.

### Changed
- **The app: a marshal's phone no longer keeps the field join key, CA-pinned scoreboard links are remembered, and buzzes reach a closed app on Android** (decisions APX-1 to APX-3; `app/`). (1) The join key stays only where it is used, by a prop (its join card) and by the phone controller; `AppSettings.joinKeyUse` records which, a settings migration (version 2) clears it on an upgrade for a phone that is a marshal and never ran the controller, and confirming a marshal clears it unless the controller's. (2) A scoreboard link with `&ca=` is kept per field by the CA's fingerprint (`scoreboard_links`, read code sealed) and offered under *Campos guardados*; it is forgotten when the CA changes. (3) While the player view is open on a live game, an Android foreground service with an ongoing notification (four languages) keeps the app's engine running, so the same event buzzes play with the app closed or the screen off; it stops when the game ends or the player leaves; no Play services; iOS is not covered. Manuals (player, marshal, en and pt) and `app/README.md` updated.
### Added
- **Site server, hosted mode: a database role per field, so a SQL injection can no longer switch fields by setting `atalaia.tenant`** (decision S21-13, decisions S2113-1 to S2113-8; `tenant_roles.py`, `tenancy.py`, `hosted.py`, migration `0023_tenant_roles`, `hosted-roles.sh`, `restore.sh`, `server/README.md`, `docs/DEPLOY.md`, ADR-P4-4's amendment of 2026-10-28): the row-level security keyed on a setting the server's own role could set. Now every field has a database role `atalaia_t_<field id>` (no login, no superuser, no `BYPASSRLS`, owns nothing) that alone holds the grants on the field's schema, tables and sequences and on its rows of the hub's sharing tables; the server's login role holds none (it reads the registry, adds to the quota inbox and `SET ROLE`s to a field's role, `WITH INHERIT FALSE`), and every request's transaction starts with `SET LOCAL ROLE` to its field's. A statement of field A that sets `atalaia.tenant` to B is refused B's schema outright, for reading and writing. `pg_catalog.set_config` is revoked from `PUBLIC` (`hosted-roles.sh`, as the superuser) so a field's role cannot switch role or setting from inside a statement; the server refuses to start while any role can call it (and `asyncpg`'s type introspection is told not to). The hub's chrono sharing keeps working across fields: its policy and functions read the caller's field from the role in use (`atalaia_caller_tenant()`), not from the setting. `hosted create`/`migrate` make and grant the roles (the owner role now needs `CREATEROLE`; `hosted-roles.sh` gives it), migration `0023_tenant_roles` makes the role of every field a hub already has (and does nothing in a single-field server), `hosted delete` drops it, `hosted check` verifies the roles, the grants and the `set_config` restriction, and a hub's restore uses `--no-acl` and `hosted migrate` for the grants (roles belong to the cluster, not to the backup). Upgrading a hub: run `hosted-roles.sh`, then the hub's `hosted migrate`, in one maintenance window (DEPLOY.md). **Not closed, and said so:** a second statement that is a `SET ROLE` command would still switch fields (PostgreSQL authorises it by the session user); the server's driver sends one statement per call, and a login role and pool per field would close it for good. Single-field mode is unchanged. New tests in `test_tenants.py`, `test_hub_share.py` and `test_migrations.py`.
- **The privacy notice is published, and accounts are asked to consent again when it changes** (decisions CONS-1 to CONS-6, S17-16; `server/atalaia_server/privacy_notice.py`, `api/privacy.py`, `web/privacy_pages.py`, `static/staff/consent.js`): the operator puts one Markdown file per language and version in a folder (`ATALAIA_PRIVACY_NOTICE_DIR`, `server/privacy-notice/` in the Compose deployment, files `privacy-notice-<lang>-<version>.md`, filled in from `docs/templates/privacy-notice-*.md`) and the server serves the files of the current `ATALAIA_PRIVACY_NOTICE_VERSION` as a public page (`/privacy`, pt-PT, English, German, Traditional Chinese, the text rendered by a small escaping Markdown renderer) and as JSON (`GET /api/v1/privacy-notice?lang=`: `version`, `lang`, `available`, `markdown`, `html`, `url`; another language of the version when the one asked for has no file, 404 `privacy_notice_not_published` when there is none). `GET /api/v1/me` gains `consent` (`status` `current`, `outdated` or `missing`, the version given, the current version, whether a notice is published); `POST /api/v1/me/consents/account` with `{notice_version}` consents again (the live consent for the older version ends, a new one is recorded, `users.gdpr_consent_at` moves, `consent_renewed` is audited) and answers 409 `notice_version_changed` with `current_version` when the version shown is not the current one; `POST /api/v1/auth/register` takes an optional `notice_version` and answers the same 409. The staff pages show a panel at the top after login (read the notice, tick, confirm) in the four languages; nothing is blocked, so the account's own export and erasure stay available. The text of the notice stays the operator's: this is only the mechanism. Tests: `tests.test_privacy_notice`.

### Removed
- **`users.data_retention_days`** (decision CONS-3; migration `0020_drop_user_retention`): an unused per-account override that was only exported and set to null on erasure. GDPR asks for a defined period per kind of data, which the daily purge applies; a field per account promised what the code did not do. The access answer and the export's `profile.json` no longer have it.
### Added
- **A box reports the points its teams lost to penalties, so the totals subtract them** (decision BSC-1, closing E6; RFC 0001 Amendment 18; core `msg_codec`, `firmware/esp32/main/box_game.c`, both controllers, `app/lib/radio/rf_codec.dart`, `protocol/schema/messages.yaml`, `protocol/vectors/state.json`). STATE gets an optional key 9, `lost`: a per-team map of the points lost to penalties on that node, a grow-only counter merged by maximum like the gains and sent only when a team lost something (a game with no penalty sends the same STATE as before). The controller's total per team is the gains minus the boxes' penalties plus the GM's adjustments, so Kill Confirmed's penalty now lowers the team's total on the controller, the console, the scoreboards, MQTT and the app (the station's own screen already showed it net). The node's `lost` is in `GET /api/v1/nodes` and kept across a controller restart. An older box sends no key (nothing changes for it); an older Pi controller skips it; **an older Android controller refuses a STATE that has it: update the app first**. The Android prop link does not carry it yet.
- **A projected score between STATEs, for modes that opt in** (decisions SL-6 and BSC-2; `mode.yaml` `scoring`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/mode.py`, both controllers; `modes/README.md` "Projected score"). A mode that scores by holding says how (`scoring: {hold: {every: tick_s, points: points_per_tick}}`, in Domination) and the controller adds the ticks a held box has made since its last STATE to the total it shows: `projected` on `GET /api/v1/games/{id}/score` and the stream's `score` message (the console writes it as an estimate). It is display only, replaced by every STATE (so it never accumulates), and the stored totals, MQTT, the sync and the final result are the boxes' reports as before; a mode without the block, a composed game, a paused game, an offline box and a box nobody holds project nothing. `GET /api/v1/controller` has `projection`, the drift of the estimate against each box's next STATE; the bench runbook for two real boards is `docs/runbooks/projected-score-bench.md` (not run yet).

### Fixed
- **Sabotage: with team codes, the defenders' code armed the bomb and the attackers' code defused it** (`modes/sabotage/node.lua`; found on the bench on 2026-10-29). Fixed roles (decision SAB-1) now hold for codes too: only the attackers' code arms, only the defenders' defuses; the other team's code shows "Not your team's code" (decision SAB-11).
- **Bomb's end screen clipped the Portuguese word** (`modes/bomb/assets/screens/defused.yaml`). DESARMADA at 48 px was wider than the 320 px screen and lost about 4 px each side; it is now drawn at 28 px, like German's and Sabotage's. New reference screen `yaml_bomb_defused_pt`.
- **The controller's default check-in notice told players their data does not go to the internet** (`controller/templates/checkin-notice-{en,pt}.md`). With a site server, the controller sends the player's pseudonymous id, the game's events and chronograph readings there (never the name or membership number). The notice now says so, says chronograph readings are kept 365 days (not "the field's retention rules"), mentions membership numbers, shared positions and the remote game master, and points to the CNPD. Found by the privacy-notice draft; its pt-PT text was also corrected (marcador, número de filiação, organização, gender-neutral wording).

### Changed
- **The Field Box's OTA app slots are 4 MB (was 2 MB), a new partition table: USB flash required once** (decision PART-1; flashed over USB, without erase, on the bench board on 2026-10-29: it logs the 4 MB layout and kept its adoption, release and mode keys): the box image was at 93 % of a 2 MB slot (1,949,520 of 2,097,152 bytes). `firmware/esp32/partitions.csv` keeps nvs (0x9000), otadata (0xf000), phy_init (0x11000) and nvs_keys (0x12000) where they were, so a box re-flashed over USB keeps its adoption, keys and settings; ota_0 stays at 0x20000 (now 0x400000 long), ota_1 is at 0x420000, and the LittleFS `storage` moved to 0x820000 (4 MB; 3.875 MB of the 16 MB flash stay unallocated). What a re-flash loses is the storage content (cached mode packages, the field's logo, the event log, a stored game to resume): it formats on the first mount (it already did on failure) and the box fetches modes and logo again. Nothing else lives there. An update over the air cannot change the partition table, so a box on the old table is flashed over USB once, **without** `erase_flash`. The new firmware compares the running table with the expected one at boot (`main/fw_layout.c`, `fw_layout_esp.c`): on another table it logs `WRONG PARTITION TABLE`, shows *Flash over USB* (en, pt, de, zh-TW), does not start the game or the radio, and an image an older firmware just put there rolls back; the modem and the pod only log it. Additive, no wire change: the Atalaia descriptor's first reserved byte is now the **layout** the image needs (`1`; old images say 0), which a box reads before it opens the slot and refuses a newer one (`layout`, a new result code in the box, the controller, the site server's shared Python, the app and the console in four languages); `tools/firmware_release.py sign` records `"layout"` in `.release.json`; the controller's slot limit is 4 MiB (an old-table box still refuses an image over 2 MiB: `too_big`). `tools/release_package.py` fails a package whose built table is not `partitions.csv`, and its README and the release notes say "USB flash required once"; `release.yml` checks the custom table. Firmware guide (en, pt) section 3.3, `firmware/esp32/README.md`, `protocol/firmware-update.md`, phase-1 STATUS. Images (0 warnings): Field Box 1,952,160 bytes, 53 % of its slot free; modem 0x8aea0 (86 %); pod 0xa4e10 (84 %).

### Added
- **A read-only demo game on the preview server** (decisions DEMO-1 to DEMO-9, the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-28 on PV-2; `server/preview/demo/`, `server/docker-compose.preview.yml`, `server/preview/preview.sh`, `docs/runbooks/preview-server.md`, `server/README.md`): so people get an idea of how the application works end to end. A new `demo-field` service runs the real field controller with three simulated Field Boxes (the Field Box firmware's own game and radio code built for Linux, with the C host modem on a UDP "air") and a director that plays Domination games forever through the controller's API: a game, then another; twelve players with made-up callsigns check in, a marshal's signed chrono readings come in (one replica over the limit), boxes are captured, scores rise, the players walk the plan and report positions, the game ends, the score stays on show, and again; every 24 games the container restarts with a fresh controller database, and any restart is clean (a game left running is ended, boxes rejoin). It is visible read-only through a proxy published on `127.0.0.1:8001` only (a second Cloudflare Tunnel hostname, the maintainer's step): the GM console with the public read token, the scoreboard, a new player's view (`/player/`: callsign, team, check-in, chrono result, clock, score, the team's positions on the plan, the latest captures) and a "Try the demo" home page, all in pt-PT, English, German and Traditional Chinese. Nothing a visitor can change: GET and HEAD only (405 before anything is forwarded), a list of read endpoints (join cards, adoption, firmware, marshals, other people's check-ins are 404), only the one public read token (the controller's GM token is made at each start and never accepted or forwarded), a one-way WebSocket, a read-only container with every capability dropped on a network with no way out; no personal data (an invented field in the open sea, invented callsigns, nothing about visitors). On the site server `python -m atalaia_server demo live` makes a field of its own for the controller's syncs (`demo-ao-vivo`, so the hourly `demo load` never touches a synced game), its API key (written once to a volume, mode 0600, only its hash in the database) and deletes all but the newest 12 games; `ATALAIA_PREVIEW_DEMO_URL` puts "Try the live demo" on the preview's landing page. `preview.sh live status|restart|reset|logs` runs it. Tests: `tools/docker_server.py preview-demo-unit` (the proxy against a stand-in controller, the plan, the callsigns: 17), `preview-demo` (a scratch copy of the whole stack, a minute a game: a game plays itself, nothing can be changed, nothing personal, the site server shows it, a clean restart), `preview-check` (now covers the demo's network, volumes and hardening), 8 new site server tests (`tests.test_preview`).

- **The I/O pod's settings can be pushed over the radio from the GM console** (the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-28 to build what decision PF15 left open; decisions PODC-1 to PODC-9; `protocol/pod-config.md`, RFC 0001 Amendment 17). Until now a pod's settings were its firmware build's Kconfig. The field controller (Pi or phone) now sends CMD pod (opcode 10, `{"v": 1, "rev", "btn", "bright", "vol", "tamper", "reset"}`, `dest` the node id of the phone the pod serves): the colours of the four buttons, the LED ring's default brightness, the volume and whether the enclosure switch reports, before a game starts or after it ended. It is an ordinary sealed game packet, so it is authenticated like every other CMD (game key, epoch, no repeats); the pod's radio takes it after that check and does not pass it on (`modem_io_t.rx_filter`, `main/pod/pod.c`), parses it strictly (an unknown key, a value out of range or an unknown colour refuses all of it), orders pushes by `rev` (a pod applies only a `rev` above the last one it kept: stale ones are ignored, a repeat is answered again), keeps the settings in its flash (NVS `pod`/`settings`, applied at boot before the first IDENT; `main/pod/pod_settings.c`), sends its IDENT again with the new colours and tells its phone with the EVT `pod.config` `{"rev"}`. The phone (`app/lib/prop/prop_session.dart`) sends that over the radio as `node.setup_applied` (`kind` `pod_config`, `rev`): the acknowledgement the boxes' scenario setups already use (Amendment 16). The controller sends the command again after 8, 16, 32, 64 and 128 s until it hears that, then shows "no answer" (the phone has no pod, an older pod firmware, out of reach). Not in it, on purpose: the radio's key, region and channel, the pins, the Bluetooth security, the admin PIN (a pod has no admin menu) and the LED count (wiring) stay in the build. Both controllers (`controller/atalaia_controller/pod_config.py`, `GET`/`DELETE /api/v1/pod-config`, `POST /api/v1/pod-config/{node}`; `app/lib/controller/controller_pod_config.dart`, the same API and a new `whoami` feature `pod_config`), the console's *I/O pods' settings* panel under the Boxes tab (pt, en, de, zh-tw), `rev` the clock's seconds or one above the last sent to that node. New: `protocol/vectors/pod_config.json` (written by `tools/pod_config_vectors.py`, checked in `check.sh` and CI; the generated `firmware/esp32/test/pod_config_vectors.h`), the same steps run by the pod's C (`test_pod_settings.c`), the Pi controller (`test_pod_config.py`) and the Android controller (`controller_pod_config_test.dart`); `test_pod.c` (taken, not passed on, a repeat, stale, another node, another key, reset), the host pod's `POD settings` line, an end-to-end test with the host pod and the Pi controller (`test_pod_e2e.py`) and one with the host pod, the phone's prop session and a host modem (`prop_pod_session_test.dart`), a console browser test. Docs: `protocol/pod-config.md`, `capability-proxy.md`, the firmware README, the I/O pod build guide's "Settings over the radio" (en, pt), the firmware guide, the controller and console READMEs. The pod image is 677,312 bytes (33 % of its 2 MB slot) with 0 warnings. In software and against the host pod; not run on a board, no radio transmission.
- **Site server: ATAK certificates last 90 days, and the staff page says when one is near expiry** (decision SVX-1, FU5-4; `ATALAIA_ATAK_CERT_DAYS`, `atalaia_server/atak_pki.py`, `services/atak.py`, `web/static/staff/atak.js`): the server still makes the device's key; a device's certificate is valid 90 days by default (was 7, 30 at most; the setting and the request may take 1 to 365 days), revocable as before. `/staff/fields/<field>/atak` shows each certificate's expiry and a warning above the table when one expires within 14 days (`expiring` on each certificate and `warn_days` in `GET /api/v1/fields/{field_id}/atak`), in pt, en, de and zh-TW. `server/README.md`, `server/docs/DEPLOY.md`.
- **Site server: chrono sharing across the tenants of a hosted hub** (decision SVX-2, S21-2; ADR-P4-4's amendment of 2026-10-28; `hub_share.py`, `api/hub_share.py`, migration `0021_hub_share_links`, `hosted.py`): fields that share chrono records now do so in hosted mode too, through a hub-level, consent-gated, read-only view, never a query across schemas. A person links their accounts at two fields with a one-time code (`POST /api/v1/me/hub-share/code`, `.../link`, `DELETE`, `GET`; nothing is matched by e-mail or user name); each field publishes only its own signed records of a linked person who consented for that field while the field's switch is on, into three tables in `public` under row-level security (own tenant's rows only); the other field reads them only through `public.atalaia_share_read`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` function that takes the caller's tenant from `atalaia.tenant`. Consent withdrawal, unlinking, erasure, a player's erasure, the retention purge and `hosted delete` remove the published copies. `hosted init` and `hosted migrate` make the tables and functions; `hosted check` verifies them. Single-field servers are unchanged (the endpoints answer 404 `not_hosted`). The privacy-notice drafts' recipients and sharing rows (`docs/templates/privacy-notice-{en,pt}.md`) name it. Tests: `server/tests/test_hub_share.py`, `test_tenants.py` (`hosted check`), `test_chrono.py`. Not built: app or staff screens for the link code.
- **How long the field keeps game data and chrono records is a field setting, and a controller's erasure reaches the site server by itself** (decisions RET-1 to RET-8, S14-10; `controller/atalaia_controller/{checkin,store,config,sync_format,sync_client}.py`, `app/lib/controller/`, `server/atalaia_server/{api/fields,sync,chrono_registry,services/branding}.py`, migration `0022_field_retention`, the console's check-in settings and the staff settings page, `protocol/sync.md`, `protocol/vectors/sync.json`). (1) **Game data:** the controller never deleted a game's events, scores and logs. `game_retention_days` (30-3653, **365 by default**) is set like the field's other settings: `[checkin] game_retention_days` on the Pi, the GM console's check-in settings (`PUT /api/v1/checkin/settings`; the phone controller too), and from the site server in `settings.game_retention_days` of the field answer (additive; the field's admin sets it with `PATCH /api/v1/fields/{id}` or the new **Data retention** section of the staff settings page, `fields.game_retention_days`). The daily purge deletes an ended game that old whole (events, scores, nodes, positions, check-in rows, token key, schedule link, sync state), and **with a sync set up never before the site server has all of it** (`sync_state.final`), so a game that cannot be sent stays. (2) **Chrono retention per field:** `fields.default_retention_days`, which only the server's purge read, can now be set (`chrono_retention_days` in the PATCH, in `field_json`, on the settings page and in the sync's `settings`) and both controllers use it for readings, other fields' readings and a marshal's earlier keys instead of a fixed 365 days (30-3653). (3) **Erasure:** the GM's erasure of a player and a player's chrono erasure are kept as erasure records (store schema 17; the phone's check-in document) and sent with the sync (`POST /api/v1/sync/erasures`, never a name); the site server erases the player id's chrono records, refused readings and replicas, and for an identity erasure the link of the id to an account, and the controller forgets the record once acknowledged (an older site server is skipped). The privacy notice template's retention table (`docs/templates/privacy-notice-{en,pt}.md`), the controllers' check-in notices (`{chrono_retention_days}` and `{game_retention_days}` are filled in), the GM manuals (en, pt), both READMEs and `protocol/` say so; shared vectors for the new settings and the erasures body run on the Pi, the phone and the site server.
- **A new built-in game mode, Sabotage (Sabotagem)** (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-28; decisions SAB-1 to SAB-10; `modes/sabotage/`, built into the Field Box as the sixteenth preset, the simulator, the app's built-in modes): the Bomb's rounds until the game time runs out. The attackers arm, the defenders defuse; an explosion is a point for the attackers, a defuse one for the defenders; the box then shows the result, rests for `pause_s` seconds and goes back to standby. At `game_time_min` (20) the game ends and the team with more points wins, equal points are a draw, and a bomb armed at that moment does not count. Fixed roles, hold times, fuse and optional keypad team codes as in the Bomb; the standard bomb events, no new event. Six screens in four languages (`standby` with the score, `countdown`, `defused`, `exploded`, `pause`, `game_over`); 13 simulator tests, Field Box picker, reference-image and OLED tests, a Dart catalog test; the box fonts gained the letters 合 壞 平.
- **Scenario objectives are active by default in squad games, and the GM chooses visible or hidden for every game** (decisions RVL-1 to RVL-5, the maintainer's answer of 2026-10-28 to SQ-2; `core/scenario/` (`manifest.lua`, `package.lua`, `luagen.lua`, `runtime.lua`), `controller/atalaia_controller/{scenario_service,engine,api}.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_{scenarios,engine}.dart`, the GM console's game form and block editor). An objective that depends on others and gives no `visibility` now compiles with `follows_reveal: true` instead of a visibility the package decides, and the *game* decides when it becomes known: the GM's choice in the new-game form (**Objectives at the start**, `objective_reveal`: `all` or `active` in `POST /api/v1/games`), else the scenario's own top-level `reveal`, else `active` in a game with squads and `all` in one without. The runtime reads it as the parameter `scenario_reveal`; the staff's `game.scenario` carries `reveal` (the game's) and `reveal_default` (the manifest's, null when it says nothing), and the players' view and the "send the game again when an objective is revealed" rule follow it. An objective's own `visibility` (`hidden` included) always wins, and a package compiled before this keeps what it compiled to. The block editor's rules block gained "as the game decides", its default. Tests: the compile vectors regenerated, a shared contract case "RVL objective reveal default", controller `RevealDefault` tests, a compiled chain fixture for the simulator, Dart `squad_objectives_test`, a console browser test. Manuals (en, pt), `core/scenario/README.md` and `modes/README.md` updated.
- **The first full draft of the privacy notice, in pt-PT and English** (documentation only; decisions PRIV-1 to PRIV-8; `docs/templates/privacy-notice-pt.md` and `-en.md`, `dpa-template.md`): rewritten from what every component does today, and marked at the top as a draft, not legal advice, not yet reviewed by a lawyer. Part A, the field's full notice in GDPR order (controller and the three ways a field runs Atalaia, purposes and legal bases, recipients, how data moves with and without internet, transfers, third parties, retention as the code applies it per place, rights, the app's local storage and Keystore, security, children, the CNPD, changes): accounts and login (password hash, TOTP, passkeys, Google, Microsoft and Apple sign-in with the provider's subject only), registration and check-in, association membership numbers and expiry, chronograph readings with repeat-offender marks, replicas and NFC or QR stickers, positions (GPS and BLE beacons; the controller, the remote GM and remote ATAK in memory only, staff ATAK), NFC player tags, game history, the remote GM, clubs, the hosted mode (the field as controller, the hub operator as processor), map tiles and the sign-in providers, and `jogarairsoft.pt` as a placeholder only. Part B, a short in-app notice for account creation (what S17-16 waits on). Part C, a short notice for the check-in poster and page. Part D, the points for the lawyer. Choices that belong to the field are marked **[ESCOLHA DO CAMPO]** / **[FIELD CHOICE]** and those for the lawyer **[ADVOGADO]** / **[LAWYER]**. The DPA template now lists the data the site server really holds, that positions pass through memory only, the operator's commands, sub-processor placeholders and the export and deletion at the end of the service. The version label is `draft-2026-10-28`; the server's default `ATALAIA_PRIVACY_NOTICE_VERSION` is unchanged.
- **A registration list can be imported from the phone controller's own screens** (decision PIM-1, the caveat of PKE-1; `app/lib/controller/controller_import_screen.dart`, `app/android/.../FilePickerPlugin.kt`, `app/lib/platform/`): until now only the GM console's import panel, served by the phone controller, could use `POST /api/v1/games/{id}/checkins/import`. The controller screen now has a **Registration list** section (while a game is live): **Choose a file** (a CSV or JSON file, with Android's own file chooser, no storage permission), the **Source**, the tick that the field has its own legal basis for the list, **Check the file** (a dry run: nothing changes, each row is listed as new, changed, skipped or refused with the reason, the columns not read are only named) and then **Import**, on the same endpoint and with the same rules as the console. A file that is not UTF-8 or over 1 MiB never leaves the phone; a refused file, row or request is told in words in Portuguese, English, German and Traditional Chinese (the console's wording). `app/README.md`, the Game Master manual (en, pt) and the phase 3 STATUS are updated; the zh-tw test font subsets were made again for the new characters. Tests: `controller_import_screen_test.dart`, `controller_import_request_test.dart`
- **The warning for names the box cannot draw covers the game's name, a team's display name and a scenario's and its objectives' names** (decision GLY-1, extending PKE-3): under the game's name, under each team's display name in the game form's team branding, and under the scenario builder's check result (the scenario's name and its objectives' names, also the Portuguese ones), the console warns with the letters, in the same words as for the team names; a warning only, the name is accepted. Console browser test `test_game_scenario_and_team_display_names_the_box_cannot_draw_are_warned_about`.
- **The staff club page lists the club's fields, and migration 0018 has a data test** (decisions SV8-1, SV8-2): `/staff/clubs/<club>` shows the fields where the club plays (the `fields` of `GET /api/v1/clubs/{id}`, decision PKC-3: a member sees every field that saved a team of the club, a field with a public page as a link to it, the others as text; a note when there are none), in the four languages, with no change to the content security policy. `tests.test_migrations.test_0018_with_data` upgrades from 0016 with chrono violations in the tables, checks the backfilled `repeat_offender` flags (per field and player, by the deciding readings' times, a tie by reading id) and the null `limit_j`/`delta_j`, then downgrades; a browser test checks the club page at 375 px.
- **The modem board's small screen speaks the controller's language** (decisions MOD-1 to MOD-4; `firmware/esp32/main/modem/`, `protocol/usb-modem.md`, both controllers). The USB radio modem's OLED showed `MODEM MODE`, `LINK`, `waiting`, `OK`, `DOWN`, `RADIO FAULT` and the rest in English only. It now has them in Portuguese, English, German and Traditional Chinese, as the Field Box's screens do: the texts are `TXM_*` entries in `box_text.c` (the box's table, so `tools/gen_box_fonts.py --check` stays green; the Chinese words use only letters the box's fonts already carry, so no font was made again). The 8-pixel font has ASCII only, so Portuguese and German use the six lines with unaccented words (`LINK PERDIDO`, `JOGO`, `FALHA NO LORA`, `FUNKFEHLER`); Chinese has a layout of its own, since a Han letter needs the 14-pixel font: the link state in the header bar, the game line a 16-pixel row, the channel, counters and duty budget as before. The modem learns the language from the controller over the serial link it already has: a new command, **`SET_LANG`** (opcode `0x09`, a language code in ASCII; additive, framing stays version 1), which the controller sends whenever the modem's new IDENT key `"lang": true` says it may, after every connect and reboot (the modem keeps it in RAM only); a modem built before this is never sent it. The Pi controller (`radio_modem.py`) and the phone controller (`serial_modem.dart`) both send the language of the boxes' screens: the `box_language` setting, or with `auto` the language of the console that created the live game. Until then, and on the boot splash, the modem uses a build option, Kconfig `ATALAIA_MODEM_LANG` (`en` by default). Tests: 6 new firmware host cases (SET_LANG, IDENT's `lang`, the language per screen state and every line of every language within 16 columns and ASCII) and 18 new reference images (`test/screens/oled/modem_*_{pt,de,zh-tw}.png`), SET_LANG against the C modem from the Pi controller, and the Pi's and the phone's sending rules. Docs: `protocol/usb-modem.md`, the firmware README, the firmware guide's modem section (en, pt) and the controller setup guide.
- **The field's logo on the box from JPEG and WebP too** (decision LOGO-1, closing PKG-2's gap; Pi and phone controllers): the controllers still convert only a PNG logo, so the GM console now decodes a JPEG or WebP logo with the browser's canvas right after the upload and makes the box-ready bytes itself (`controller/console/src/components/box-logo.js`: the same whole-integer area average, alpha on `#101418`, `ALG1` 96 x 96 RGB565, 18,440 bytes as `protocol/mode-packages.md` section 8; every case of `protocol/vectors/box_logo.json` but the partly transparent `rgba8_square` gives the vector's SHA-256 in Chromium). It uploads them to the new GM endpoint `PUT /api/v1/field/branding/logo/box?id=<logo id>` on both controllers (additive; checked for size and header, kept next to the branding for that logo only, `409` for another logo, a PNG logo or site-managed branding), and `GET /api/v1/field/box-logo[/release]` serve them when the logo is not a PNG, so boxes need no change. Tests: `controller/tests/test_box_logo.py`, `app/test/controller/controller_box_logo_upload_test.dart`, two console browser tests (a JPEG logo, then WebP, then PNG; the vectors). Docs: protocol, controller README, GM and Field Box manuals (en, pt).
- **Scenario objectives (and the scenario's own name, description and parameter labels) can be named in German and Traditional Chinese** (decision PKB-9's follow-up, SCL-1; `core/scenario/manifest.lua`, `controller/console/src/components/scenario-blocks.js`): a manifest's `i18n` takes `pt`, `de` and `zh-tw` (it took only `pt`), so a scenario whose `lang` is `de` or `zh-tw` sends each box the objective's German or Chinese name instead of the English one (the compiler already picked `i18n.<lang>.name`; only the validator refused the key, `bad_lang`). The console's block editor has a *name in German / Chinese* and a *description in German / Chinese* block for the scenario and for each objective, and a second row on a parameter for its German and Chinese labels, and round-trips them; an older manifest (`i18n.pt` alone) gives the same blocks and the same YAML as before, and a language outside `pt`, `de` and `zh-tw` (`fr`, `en`) is still refused by the validator and named as not shown by the blocks. New: a shared valid case in `protocol/vectors/scenario.json` (`scenario_compile.json` regenerated; the Android controller reads both, and uses the same core), a console round-trip test (`test_names_in_german_and_chinese_round_trip_and_old_manifests_stay`), five console texts in each of the four languages. Not changed: a box's font still draws a Chinese name only as far as it has the letters (`?` for the rest), and a Chinese name takes three bytes a letter of the setup command's 127.
- **The association-membership follow-ups** (decisions APDF-1 to APDF-6; `protocol/apd.md`, `protocol/vectors/registration_import.json` and `controller_api.json`): (1) the registration import has two more optional columns, `apd_id` and `membership_expires`, beside `federation_number` (the shared `import_format.py`, which the site server re-exports, its Dart port `registration_import.dart`, the site server's and both controllers' imports); the site server's registration export writes them too, so an export imports back (the Pi's and the phone's check-in CSV already had them), and an older file without them imports as before; (2) the app's marshal **Check in by hand** form asks for the APD, number and expiry when the field asks for a membership (the existing `apd_id`, `membership_number` and `membership_expires` keys of `POST /api/v1/games/{id}/checkins`); (3) a `pending` player with no declared replica is listed in `GET /api/v1/games/{id}/chrono-list`, so the marshal's queue shows their verdict and **Let in** (the Pi and the phone controller; the counts stay the replicas'); (4) the Pi's chrono-list rows carry `apd_id`, `membership_number`, `membership_expires`, `apd_override` and `apd_status` (the phone's already did), held to a new shared contract case; (5) a controller database upgrade test from store schema 14 (rows with federation numbers) to the current one (`controller/tests/test_store.py`). Manuals (marshal, game master; en and pt) and the integrations guide updated
- **Architecture diagrams in Portuguese** (documentation only): `docs/architecture.pt.md` is the pt-PT version of `docs/architecture.md`. Its seven Mermaid diagrams are the Portuguese project-page diagrams (the templates filled with `site/i18n/pt.yaml`) and the "what flows / what does not flow" texts follow the page; the rest is translated. The two files link to each other, and `docs/README.md` and the manuals' related guides link both.
- **The architecture diagrams on the project page** (decisions SITEARCH-1 to SITEARCH-6): a page "How it fits together" (`/como-se-liga/`, `/en/how-it-fits/`, `/de/zusammenspiel/`, `/zh-tw/how-it-fits/`) shows the seven diagrams of `docs/architecture.md` as plain SVG in Portuguese, English, German and Traditional Chinese, each with a short "what flows / what does not" text. The Mermaid diagrams are templates (`site/src/diagrams/arch-*.mmd`, labels in `site/i18n/*.yaml`) drawn by the new `tools/render_diagrams.py` (Docker); the SVGs follow the reader's light or dark setting, and `python3 tools/build_site.py --check` fails when a template, a label or the tool changed and the SVGs were not drawn again. In `docs/architecture.md` the bold box titles are now Mermaid markdown strings, so that the SVG shows them bold (GitHub draws them the same).
- **Architecture diagrams** (documentation only; English, Portuguese follows): `docs/architecture.md` shows with seven Mermaid diagrams how the parts of Atalaia talk to each other: the whole system with the links that work with no internet marked, the radio layers, a game from the invite to the end, players on game day, the controller and site server sync (and what happens offline), getting firmware, modes and the field key onto a box with the trust chain, and the site server's login. Every arrow was checked against the protocol documents and the code; the places where the design specification and the code differ are listed at the end of the page. Linked from `docs/README.md`, the top-level `README.md` and the manuals' related guides.
- **Releases are prepared by a pull request** (decisions PREP-1 to PREP-8): **Run workflow** on `.github/workflows/prepare-release.yml` (`bump` patch/minor/major, or an explicit `version`, and an optional `prerelease` label) opens the pull request "Release vX.Y.Z" with CHANGELOG's Unreleased section moved under the new version, the version files bumped as release 0.4.0's commit did by hand and the notes as its description; merging it makes `.github/workflows/tag-release.yml` tag the merge commit and start `release.yml` for the tag (a tag pushed with the workflow token starts no workflow), then open a pull request that sets the next development version. `tools/prepare_release.py` (standard library, with `controller/tests/test_prepare_release.py`) does the editing and refuses an empty Unreleased section. `release.yml` treats a run on a tag `vX.Y.Z` as a real release whatever started it (a branch run stays a dry run), refuses other tags, and stops early when the release exists. "Cutting a release" in `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### Fixed
- **The GM console's check-in poster form keeps what the GM typed until it is saved** (audit CF-1 of every console form, after the check-in settings fix `818d0616`; `controller/console/src/components/checkin-panel.js`): the addresses, the Wi-Fi name and the "pinned to this game" tick were drawn from the saved poster, so any new drawing of the tab (a player checking in, the console's clock) put the saved values back and the GM's typing was lost. They now go into a draft, cleared by a save (a browser test checks a player in mid-edit; it failed on the old code). The audit went through every other form of the console (24 components, the field map, the poster, label and scoreboard pages): the rest already keep their edits in state, act at once on a change, or hold no saved value; none needed a change.

## [0.4.0] - 2026-10-28

The first release published on GitHub, a **pre-alpha, not field-tested** pre-release: firmware images for the Field Box (and the Satellite, the same image), the controller's USB radio modem and the I/O pod, as a USB flash bundle, a single merged image for browser flashers, and the app image with its signed `.release.json`, built and signed by the release workflow (`.github/workflows/release.yml`) with the CI release key. Building the firmware yourself still works and is still documented (`docs/manuals/en/firmware.md`).

### Added
- **Firmware releases on GitHub, signed with two release keys** (the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-28, decisions REL-1 to REL-8): a pushed tag `v*` builds the Field Box, modem and pod images with the project's two mode keys and two release keys as defaults and no bench options, and creates a GitHub pre-release with, for each image, `atalaia-<variant>-<version>-usb.zip` (bootloader, partition table, app, `flash_args`, README), `atalaia-<variant>-<version>-merged.bin` (for browser flashers, at offset 0), `atalaia-<variant>-<version>.bin` with `atalaia-<variant>-<version>.bin.release.json` (what a controller or a box checks, `tools/firmware_release.py`), and `SHA256SUMS`. The **root** release key (`424d053af5cdbc35`) is held offline by the maintainer; the **CI** release key (`32b3c815ac30ad43`) is a repository secret and signs the releases. Both are the defaults of `CONFIG_ATALAIA_OTA_RELEASE_KEYS` (and `tools/docker_fw.py --release-keys`), the Pi controller's and the phone controller's `[firmware] release_keys`. `firmware/esp32/docs/RELEASE_SIGNING.md` has what to do if the CI key leaks; `docs/community/keys.md` lists the keys.
- **The hardware build manuals are brought up to date, with a firmware guide for builders and a controller setup guide** (documentation only; English, Portuguese follows): `docs/manuals/en/firmware.md` (the four images, how to get one today, flashing with esptool on Windows, macOS and Linux with `write_flash "@flash_args"` at 921600 baud and the `--after hard_reset chip_id` restart, the build and file options, first boot and the setup page, adoption by code, updates over the air, recovery, the modem board and the I/O pod) and `docs/manuals/en/controller-setup.md` (parts, the modem board, install, first start, the console address, backups for a Raspberry Pi controller). `hardware/field-box/BUILD.md` gains a pin reference (signal, GPIO, firmware option, wire colour, destination, voltage; section 15), checked against Kconfig by `tools/check_wiring.py`; the Satellite's and the pod's pin tables gain the same columns; the target addon page gains its port's pin reference. Fixed as stale against the firmware: the settings file of a Field Box build is `firmware/esp32/build/sdkconfig`, not `firmware/esp32/sdkconfig`; the flash baud is 921600; a PCF8574A is found by the firmware (the diagram said "not PCF8574A", re-rendered); the LED ring starts at 40 % brightness; the first screens of a new box. Things not yet tried on a real board are marked as such.
- **The project's mode signing keys** (the maintainer's choice of 2026-10-28): an offline **root** key (`d9521a79030ce544`, held by the maintainer) and a **CI** key (`7275108d2e3fe583`, the secrets of the registry repository, used by its new *Verified badge* workflow, which a maintainer starts by hand and which opens a pull request). Both are in the registry's `keys/project/` and are now the defaults of the Field Box firmware's `CONFIG_ATALAIA_MODE_KEYS` (and `tools/docker_fw.py --mode-keys`), the Android app's built-in mode keys (props) and the controllers' `[modes] box_keys` (Pi and phone); a field still trusts them for its own installs only by listing them in `[modes] keys`. `docs/community/keys.md` lists them, why the CI key is a secret, and what to do if it is exposed.
- **A staff page for a field's settings** (decisions FS-1 to FS-6; `server/`). `/staff/fields/<field>/settings` (the field's `admin`, linked from the field's sections) sets what only `PATCH /api/v1/fields/{field_id}` reached: the public page, the check-in rules, the membership rule (accepted list or any, register, check-in, expiry, the country's shipped list and the field's own entries), chrono sharing and the country. The membership words (APD or association) follow the country and change on the page, with the offered list, when a new country is saved; every refusal has words in pt-PT, English, German and Traditional Chinese; the page tells that the controllers get the change at once. No API change; GM manual (en, pt) and the server README say where to set them.
- **APD membership becomes association membership, with the field's country** (the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-28; package H, decisions MEM-1 to MEM-6; migration `0019_field_country`; `protocol/apd.md`, `protocol/vectors/apd.json` and `sync.json`, `registry/apds/`): players in other countries may need a membership too (a club, a federation, an insurance body), just not an "APD". Each entry of the shipped list (`registry/apds/apds.json`, schema and file keep their historical names) has a `country` (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2; the three shipped are `PT`). A **field has a country**: on the site server (`fields.country`, `PATCH /api/v1/fields/{id}` `country`, in the field's JSON, `fields-by-slug`, the registration list and `settings.country` of `GET /api/v1/sync/field?settings=1`, additive; `GET /api/v1/apds?country=`) and on both controllers (`[checkin] country`, the console's settings, the `country` check-in setting of `PUT /api/v1/checkin/settings`, the phone controller's; 409 when the site's applies with `[sync] branding = "site"`); the default is `PT` where the time zone is Europe/Lisbon, Atlantic/Azores or Atlantic/Madeira (the existing fields too, by the migration), otherwise unset. **Wording by country:** a PT field keeps the "APD" texts; any other country or none says "association membership" / "membership number" in pt-PT (gender-neutral), English, German and Traditional Chinese, in the app (registration, check-in, marshal), the GM console, the phone controller, the site server's staff pages and the public game page. **Which list a field offers:** the entries of its country plus its own `custom` ones (none from the shipped list without a country); the accepted-list rule is unchanged. Wire names, verdict codes and error codes stay (`apd_id`, `apd`, `apd_status`, ...: historical, documented in `protocol/apd.md`); a client treats an answer with no `country` key as `PT`. Shared vectors gained `country_valid`, `country_invalid`, `default_country`, `wording` and `choices` (a non-PT field) and `settings.country` cases, run by the server, the Pi controller, the phone controller and the app. Tests: `server/tests/test_apd_membership.py` (`CountryTests`), `test_roles.py`, `test_migrations.py`, the staff browser test, `controller/tests/test_apd.py`, the console's browser tests, `app/test/protocol/apd_test.dart`, `app/test/controller/controller_apd_test.dart`, the check-in, site and marshal widget tests, `registry/tests/test_apds.py`. Manuals (game master, marshal, player; en, pt) say what changes outside Portugal.
- **Android props fetch signed mode packages from their controller, as Field Boxes do** (the item P5B-17 and P5D-1 left for "a later PE24 slice"; decisions PKF-1 to PKF-8; `app/lib/modes/prop_mode_fetch.dart`, `app/lib/prop/prop_setup.dart`, `app/lib/ui/settings_screen.dart`): when a join names a mode or modifier the app does not carry, asks for a fetch (join key 16) and names the controller (keys 13 and 14), the prop downloads each missing part over HTTPS pinned to the field CA, checks it with the box's own checks (`boxCheckRelease` and `boxCheckArchive`, the shared `box_checks` vectors: the release's exact form, the Ed25519 signature against the prop's mode keys, kind, tier, never a built-in id, size, SHA-256, the canonical archive, then its mode.yaml agreeing with the signed manifest and the script loading in the core's sandbox), keeps it (at most 8 packages and 512 KiB, the least recently used dropped, each checked again with the current keys every time it is read) and reports to the controller with the game key's HMAC, so its start guard stops waiting. An unsigned or badly signed package is refused with a message naming the reason and never runs. The trust roots are the keys built into the build (`--dart-define=ATALAIA_MODE_KEYS`) and a new setting, *Mode keys for fetched modes* (at most 4), never a key a controller sends. Texts in pt, en, de and zh-tw.
- **The field's logo on the Field Box's big screen** (decision PKG-1, the maintainer's 2026-10-28 answer to BR24's "left for later"; `protocol/mode-packages.md` section 8, `protocol/vectors/box_logo.json`, `tools/box_logo_vectors.py`; additive on the wire, no join key, no frame and no partition change): both controllers convert the field's **PNG** logo for the box (`controller/atalaia_controller/box_logo.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_box_logo.dart`: decoded with no image library, fitted into 96 x 96 pixels, alpha composited on the box's screen colour, written as 18,440 bytes of RGB565; whole-integer sums, so the Pi and the phone give the same bytes) and serve it without a token at `GET /api/v1/field/box-logo/release` (the logo's id, and the SHA-256 and size of the bytes) and `GET /api/v1/field/box-logo`.
