The box fetches it once per joined game over the Wi-Fi and pinned HTTPS it already uses for mode packages (never over LoRa; `main/box_logo.c`, `box_logo_esp.c`), keeps it in `/littlefs/logo.bin` under the logo's id (so it is fetched again only when the logo changes; a failed or cut fetch keeps what it had, a controller with no logo removes it, adoption to another field forgets it) and draws it, with no decoder in the firmware, on the **READY** screen and on the bomb's waiting screen; the Atalaia towers of the OLED, scaled up, take its place when the box keeps none (the `ready` reference screens change), and the OLED keeps the Atalaia tower. The Field Box image grew by 3,328 bytes, to 1,949,264 of its 2,097,152-byte OTA slot (92.95 %). A JPEG or WebP logo is not converted (a controller has no decoder for them): those boxes keep the Atalaia tower. Tests: `controller/tests/test_box_logo.py`, `app/test/controller/controller_box_logo_test.dart`, the contract cases of `controller_api.json`, `firmware/esp32/test/test_box_logo.c` and five new reference screens.
- **Library and schedule changes reach the controllers on the stream at once; chrono violations get `delta_j` and `repeat_offender`; a remote GM sees the players' positions** (decisions PKD-1 to PKD-6, closing S20-8's "pushing the library instead of the next cycle", S14-7's `repeat_offender` and `delta_j`, and S19-7's positions, which the maintainer decided on 2026-10-28 should be seen "always" by a remote GM; migration `0018_chrono_violation_delta`, controller store schema 16; `protocol/sync.md`, `protocol/vectors/sync.json`; the Pi's controller, the phone's and the site server, additive on the wire): the `field.changed` note on the stream gets an optional `what` (`branding`, `library`, `schedule`) and the site server sends it after a scenario or preset or scheduled game changes; both controllers pull what changed at once, and open the stream with `[sync] library` on (the default) too. The chrono push carries the class limit (`limit_j`, only when known, and left out for a site server that refuses it) from which the site server works out each violation's `delta_j` and flags `repeat_offender`. With `[sync] remote_gm` on, whenever players share their position with the field controller, a new `positions` message sends it to the site server, which relays it in memory (never stored, never logged) to the remote GM page, which lists the players; the app's position-sharing notice says the remote GM may see it (four languages); manuals updated.
- **The site server's staff data page, quota use, per-field hosted limits, custom host names and smaller fixes** (decisions PKC-1 to PKC-8; `server/`). `/staff/fields/<field>/data` ("Dados e limites", the field's `admin`): the field's data export (`GET .../export`) read with the bearer token and offered from a `blob:` address with its SHA-256 shown, and the field's use of each hosted limit from a new read-only `GET /api/v1/fields/{id}/quotas` (four languages, CSP unchanged, `no-store`, `noindex`, 375 px). Score timelines are in game time, pauses not counted: a `score.delta` keeps its own game time, a counter's rise is placed from the arrival times and game times of the event batches around it (`services/history.py`, `game_clock`); `at` stays the arrival time. `GET /api/v1/clubs/{id}` has `fields` (where a club plays, from the teams the fields saved for it; anyone but a member sees only fields with a public page) and the public club page lists them. The staff game page shows nothing of a game under another field's address (the timeline answer now carries the game's `field_id`). Hosted mode: **per-field quotas** set by the operator in the registry over the hub's defaults (`hosted quota SLUG NAME VALUE|off|default --reason`, `hosted usage`), a **stored-bytes quota** (`ATALAIA_QUOTA_STORED_MB`, 2048 MiB; checked on the sync, images, scenarios and registration imports), and **notes for the operator** when a field is refused (`public.atalaia_quota_hits`, insert-only for the server's role; `hosted quota-hits` moves them to the hub audit log; `hosted list` and `hosted check` count them; no e-mail). **Custom host names per hosted field**: one name besides `<slug>.<domain>`, set with `hosted domain set`, turned on only by `hosted domain verify` after a DNS TXT challenge (`_atalaia-challenge.<host>`, read by a stdlib resolver, `dnsquery.py`), routed by the tenant middleware, its certificate issued by Caddy through the `ask` endpoint only for a registered, verified, active field's name (`Caddyfile.hosted` has a second site block); documented in `server/docs/DEPLOY.md`. `hosted migrate` brings an older hub's registry up to date and a server that finds the older one refuses to start. No tenant-schema migration. The hub-level OIDC callback relay is written as a design only (`docs/phase-4-site-server/ADR.md`, ADR-P4-13). Tests: `tests/test_hosted_registry.py` (new), `test_games`, `test_clubs`, `test_staff_pages`, `test_tenants`, `tests_browser/test_staff_browser.py`.
- **The app's remaining secrets are sealed with the Android Keystore** (decision PD6's open items, built 2026-10-28; decision PKA-4; `app/lib/storage/app_store.dart`): the phone controller's site API key, the player's secret at each field and the site account's refresh token join the join key, the marshal's seed and the read code in `secrets.enc`; the plaintext of an earlier version is migrated on the first start, and a failing Keystore never writes a secret to disk in the clear.
- **The app logs in without a password, and has an account screen for the login methods** (the item the server's LG-* decisions left for "a later task", built 2026-10-28; decisions PKA-1 to PKA-6; `app/lib/site/site_auth.dart`, `account_screen.dart`, `BrowserPlugin.kt`, `PasskeyPlugin.kt`, `server/atalaia_server/api/app_links.py`; in software and fakes, **not run against a real provider, a real Credential Manager or a verified App Link**): a social login (Google, Microsoft, Apple) in a Custom Tab with the site's own `https://<host>/app/oidc?s=<nonce>` as `return_to`, the `#oidc=<ticket>` return and `POST /auth/oidc/complete`; passkeys through Android's Credential Manager (Android 14 and newer); the account screen (link, unlink, passkeys, password, TOTP, the confirmation, every refusal in words in four languages). Server: `/.well-known/assetlinks.json` and `/.well-known/apple-app-site-association` from `ATALAIA_APP_ANDROID_PACKAGE`, `_SHA256` and `ATALAIA_APP_IOS_APP_IDS` (off when unset), `safe_return_to` widened to the named path `/app/oidc`, the origin `android:apk-key-hash:...` accepted for passkeys, and a `browser_url` in the `start` answer (the app's own browser must get the binding cookie, which the app's HTTP call cannot set there) that asks before a link or a reauth. The Android build names the host App Links open for (`-Patalaia.appLinkHost`). In-app account creation still waits for the privacy notice (S17-16).
- **A grid on the player's field plan** (MF-92's "the player's view of the grid waits for the plan in the app", which exists since MF-40; decision PKA-2; `app/lib/position/field_plan.dart`, `app/lib/player/field_map.dart`): the level's grid is drawn over the plan in the *Field map* card and the player's own cell is said ("C4").
- **A marshal binds an NFC sticker when registering a declared replica** (PE16's open item; decision PKA-3; `app/lib/marshal/chrono_queue.dart`): hold the sticker to the phone and its UID is the tag, or choose the QR label as before.
- **A scenario's setup commands are sent again until the box says it took them, and the box says so** (the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-28 to build the box ACK that decisions SL-13 and SL-39 left open; decisions PKB-1 to PKB-3; `core/scenario/runtime.lua`, `modes/objectives/node.lua`, RFC 0001 Amendment 16): the radio model puts 0-78 % of a one-shot setup CMD at its box, so a scenario could start with boxes never told their job. The objectives package now sends the new standard event `node.setup_applied` (id 56, normal priority, data `kind`) each time it takes a `setup`; it rides the existing ACK and resend of events (no new frame, no new opcode, the CMD is unchanged on the air). The scenario's runtime sends a setup again after 8, 16, 32, 64 and 128 s (the next second's tick) while it has not heard that event for the same `kind` from that objective's node, five times at most, each repeat carrying the state it knows now (the reboot path's `holder`, `have`, `armed`/`left`), so a repeat to a box that already has it changes nothing; a box that never says it (an older firmware) gets the same five repeats and then none. Measured in the simulator's radio model (`python -m atalaia_sim.relay_report --setup-study`, Foxhound, five seeds, the built rules `A_backoff` and `H_paced`): setups reaching their box go from 0-78 % to 100 % in the meshes and the CQB layout up to 20 boxes (89-100 % at 30) and the long field of 10 (56 % and 11 % in the long field of 20 and 30), at 1.4-2.8 CMD frames per setup instead of six for the repeats alone; the table is in `docs/phase-2-field-network/STATUS.md`. New: `simulator/atalaia_sim/relay_study.py` (`--cmd-mode oneshot|resend|resend_ack`), `relay_report.py` (`--setup-study`), the event in `protocol/schema/events.yaml` and its vector in `protocol/vectors/events.yaml` (C and Python codecs), the hand-written Foxhound example's `field.lua` and the compiled runtime (`scenario_compile.json` regenerated), tests in `simulator/tests/test_scenario.py`, `controller/tests/test_scenarios.py` and the Field Box's `test_builtin_presets.c`. The Pi and the phone controllers are unchanged (the event is an ordinary one to them).
- **The Field Box's admin menu can move an adopted box to another field by its pairing code** (decision AD-16's "left for later", built as PKB-4 in software; host tests only, **not run on a board or on the radio**): the picker's last entry **Change field** (en; *Mudar de campo*, *Feld wechseln*, 綁定新場地; after **Wi-Fi setup**) shows only on an adopted box with a radio and an admin PIN, sits behind the admin PIN like every entry and asks once more (`#` continues, `*` goes back; new `start_confirm` variant `{"adopt":true}` on the TFT and the OLED, four languages, eight new reference images). Confirmed, the box arms one move (`adoption_allow_move`) and shows a pairing code as an unadopted box does. Nothing is forgotten when it is armed: the box keeps its field and key until a KEY authenticates and opens and the new key is stored (the result is `readopted`); the PIN typed for the menu stands for the PIN of that one offer, which is the pairing code's alone (a field card or `@adopt` for another field still asks for it), and the arming ends with the code (`*`, five minutes, the lock), with the first success or with any other adoption, and a PIN wait refuses it. No wire change. `firmware/esp32/main/preset_ui.c`, `adoption.c`, `box_pair.c`, `shell.c`, `box_text.c`, `screen_renderer.c`, `oled_view.c`; tests `test_preset_ui.c`, `test_adoption.c`, `test_box_pair.c` (the vectors' whole run on an adopted box), `test_screens.c`, `test_oled_screens.c`; `protocol/adoption.md`, the Field Box manual (en, pt) and `firmware/esp32/README.md`. Image sizes: box 0x1db150 bytes of its 0x200000-byte OTA slot (7 % free), modem 0x86ba0, pod 0xa4830; 0 warnings.
- **A scenario's `lang` accepts `de` and `zh-tw`** (decision P5H-9's follow-up, PKB-9; `core/scenario/manifest.lua`): the validator, the compiler (the setup sends the language, which the objectives package's templates already had since P5H-12), the console's scenario blocks (a language dropdown with four languages; four consoles' texts) and the shared vectors (`scenario.json`: two new valid cases; `scenario_compile.json` regenerated) take `pt`, `en`, `de` and `zh-tw`. Old manifests are unchanged. A scenario's texts are still English with `i18n.pt` only (the block editor has no block for another language's texts), so a German or Chinese box shows the objective's English name; and a Chinese name would show only as far as the box's font has the letters.
- **A registration list imported into the controller's check-in list from the GM console, the grid cell in ATAK remarks and a printable grid map, a warning for names the box cannot draw, and a "from the site server" label** (package E of the 2026-10-28 follow-ups; decisions PKE-1 to PKE-8, closing F23's "small follow-up", MF-92's ATAK and printing items, P5K-5's console warning and S18-11's two console items; Pi controller, phone controller and GM console; no radio, no bench). **Import** (`POST /api/v1/games/{id}/checkins/import`; console: *Importar uma lista de inscrições*): for a field with no site server, a CSV or JSON list in the site server's import format (`display_name`, `external_ref`, `team_preference`, `federation_number`, `replicas`; other columns only named) becomes the game's *expected* check-ins; a dry run first (every row new, changed, unchanged, skipped or refused with the reason; nothing written), then a real import that needs the field to confirm it has its own basis for the list; rows upsert by source + `external_ref`, a player who has arrived is never changed, the same file again changes nothing, and the imported players have no ticket (a marshal ticks them by name) and never go back to a site server. The format file moved to `controller/atalaia_controller/import_format.py` (the site server re-exports it, so both read a list the same way) and the phone controller got a Dart port (`app/lib/controller/registration_import.dart`), both held to `protocol/vectors/registration_import.json` (CSV reading as Python's `csv` module, strict) and to the contract case in `controller_api.json`. **ATAK and print**: a box, a map feature (at its pin) and an indoor player on a level with a field grid say `grid C4` in their remarks (both controllers, `atak_xml.json`), a changed grid sends everything again, and *Imprimir o mapa com a grelha* on the Field tab prints the plan under its grid with a list of what is in which cell through the browser's print dialog. **Names the box cannot draw**: `tools/gen_box_fonts.py` now also writes `controller/console/src/box-glyphs.json` (the Latin ranges and each font's Chinese letters; `--check` fails when it is stale, `--tables` rewrites the tables without the fonts' tools) and the console warns, with the letters, under a game's team names, a game preset's name, a saved team's name and the field's name. **From the site server**: with `[sync] branding = "site"` the field branding answer carries `"source": "site"` and the Settings tab says so, shows the name and accent with no form, and lists the site's saved teams apart and read-only. Tests: `controller/tests/test_checkin_import.py`, `test_import_vectors.py`, `test_atak.py`, `test_sync_branding.py`, `simulator/tests/test_i18n_check.py`, the Dart `registration_import_test.dart`, `controller_atak_test.dart`, `controller_sync_test.dart`, the contract tests of both controllers and four new console browser tests. Docs: `controller/README.md`, `controller/console/README.md`, `protocol/checkin.md`, the game master's manual (en, pt).
- **The staff account page `/staff/account`** (decisions ACC-1 to ACC-8; `server/atalaia_server/web/templates/staff/account.html`, `static/staff/account.js`, linked from every staff page's header). Until now enrolling a passkey or linking a Google, Microsoft or Apple login was possible only through the API. The page is built on the existing endpoints only and holds no rule: the list of login methods (`GET /api/v1/me/login-methods`, `GET /api/v1/auth/methods`); link a provider (the OpenID Connect start with `purpose` `link`, back to the page) and unlink one; add (WebAuthn in the page), rename and remove passkeys; set, change or remove the password where the server allows it; TOTP status with turning it on (secret and `otpauth` address shown once, confirmed with a code) and off; and the confirmation a sensitive change asks for: the current password sent with the change, or for an account with none a TOTP code, a passkey or the provider again (`purpose` `reauth`), good for ten minutes. Every refusal is shown in words (`last_login_method`, `second_factor_required_by_role`, `totp_required_by_role`, `password_login_disabled`, `confirmation_required`, a wrong password or code, the password policy, the provider's errors). Four languages, `no-store`, `noindex`, bearer token, content security policy unchanged, usable at 375 px. Tests: `tests/test_account_page.py`, `tests_browser/test_account_browser.py` (a virtual authenticator, the fake provider).
- **The site server's login methods: social login (Google, Microsoft, Apple), passkeys, optional passwords and a passkey as the staff second factor** (the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-28; decisions LG-1 to LG-9; migration `0015_login_methods`; `server/atalaia_server/oidc.py`, `passkeys.py`, `api/login_methods.py`, the staff login page and `stepup.js`). *Social login* is OpenID Connect's authorization code flow with PKCE, state and nonce, the ID token validated in full (RS256 signature against the provider's JWKS, cached; issuer, audience, expiry, nonce); each provider is off until its `ATALAIA_OIDC_<PROVIDER>_*` settings are given (Apple's client secret is a JWT signed with the operator's `.p8` key file); only the provider and its opaque subject are stored, no email and no profile; a first login with an unknown subject makes an account under the registration rules (consent, per-address limit) and **nothing is ever linked by email**; a logged-in user links and unlinks providers. *Passkeys* (the `webauthn` library, pinned with its hashes): register, log in without a username, list, name and remove, with the RP ID the field's host name, user verification required and no attestation. *`ATALAIA_PASSWORD_LOGIN=off`* turns password login and registration off; an account always keeps one way to log in. *Staff roles* (admin, gm, marshal, server administrators) now count in a session with TOTP **or** a passkey login or step-up; a social login alone never does and is offered the TOTP or passkey step-up. Every method, link, unlink, step-up and passkey change is audit-logged; the personal-data access, export, erasure and purge cover the new rows. The staff login page has the provider buttons, a passkey button and a consent box, in four languages, with the content security policy unchanged. The app's own site login is not changed (its API needs are in the phase-4 STATUS).
- **APD membership checks for registration and check-in** (maintainer's decision of 2026-10-28: no national directory of APDs, teams, fields or events, which may come through jogarairsoft.pt later; decisions APD-1 to APD-45; migration `0016_apd_membership`; `protocol/apd.md`, `protocol/vectors/apd.json`, `registry/apds/`): in Portugal an airsoft player belongs to an APD (Associação Promotora de Desporto) recognised for airsoft and holds a membership number with an expiry. A **list of APDs** ships as data (`registry/apds/apds.json`, schema `registry/schema/apds.schema.json`, how to add one by pull request in its README; started on 2026-10-28 from the partners jogarairsoft.pt shows: ALA, APACORP and APAP, each cited from its own site; ADAPT and T13 left out until their own pages can be read) and a field can add its own entries. A **field's rule** (accepted APDs or any, membership needed to register, checked at check-in, expiry checked, its own APDs) is set by the field's admin on the site server (`PATCH /api/v1/fields/{id}` `apd`; `GET /api/v1/apds`), reaches controllers in `GET /api/v1/sync/field?settings=1` (`settings.apd`, BR24's check-in settings, with the same `field.changed` note when it changes) and `GET /api/v1/sync/roster` (additive; the old `federation_number` keeps working and is the membership number with the APD unknown) and is set on a controller without a site server in the Pi's `[checkin] apd_*` settings and console and the phone controller's settings (`[sync] branding = "site"` takes the site's; no separate switch). The **player** declares APD, number and expiry in the app's registration and check-in screens (the site server API takes `apd_id`, `membership_number`, `membership_expires`); the **marshal** sees a verdict (valid, expired, not accepted, missing) in the GM console's check-in table, the phone controller and the marshal's list, a self check-in that is not valid waits for a marshal and a marshal's confirmation is never blocked (logged without the number). The membership is personal data with the federation number's life (30 days after the game, erasure, export; never in a log). Shared vectors run on the server, the Pi controller, the phone controller and the app. Texts in pt-PT, English, German and Traditional Chinese; manuals (en, pt) for players, marshals and GMs. Tests: `server/tests/test_apd_membership.py`, `test_migrations.py`, `controller/tests/test_apd.py`, the console's browser test, `app/test/protocol/apd_test.dart`, `app/test/controller/controller_apd_test.dart`, `registry/tests/test_apds.py`.
- **The Field Box's game-timer bar** (ADR-P1-3's last open item, built 2026-10-27; `firmware/esp32/main/screen_renderer.c`, `oled_view.c`, `shell.c`): while a stand-alone game with a time limit runs, the TFT has a 6 px strip along its bottom edge, on LVGL's top layer so every C screen and every mode template keeps it, filled by the share of the game left and red in the last minute (`screen_timer_bar`), and the OLED's header shows the time left at its right where the screen has nothing else (`oled_text_timer`, `oled_view_set_timer_reader`). A game with no limit, a field game (its controller ends it) and a finished game have none. The shell reads the runner's timer from the display task and the bar draws only when the second changes. Four new screen reference images (`test/screens/timer_bar_*.png`; the others are unchanged), host tests for the bar and the OLED clock; the Field Box manual says so in English and Portuguese. Also removed the unused `espressif/esp_lvgl_port` dependency, and brought the phase 1 STATUS list up to date (the captive portal's DNS and the OTA slots were done already). Not run on a real panel.
- **The app follows a game from the scoreboard's QR code when the controller serves HTTPS with its own CA** (decision PD13's follow-up, built 2026-10-27; decisions SQR-1 to SQR-4; `app/lib/player/field_feed.dart`, `app/lib/checkin/field_trust.dart`, `controller/atalaia_controller/api.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_api.dart`): a phone controller's link is `https://...` and the app's WebSocket failed on its self-signed certificate. The link now carries the field CA's SHA-256 in the fragment (`#token=<read code>&ca=<64 hex>`, never sent to the server); the app fetches `GET /api/v1/controller/ca`, checks it against `ca` (`FieldTrust.forLink`) and follows the stream trusting that CA alone, for that stream only (nothing is kept). A `ca` that is not 64 hex digits is a bad link; plain HTTP links and links without `ca` work as before. `GET /api/v1/scoreboard-link` emits it on both controllers (the Pi keeps its `http` links first, then the poster's `https` ones with `ca`; a shared case in `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`). Tests: `app/test/player/field_feed_test.dart`, `app/test/controller/controller_host_test.dart` (a real HTTPS stream, pinned), `controller/tests/test_checkin_poster.py`, the console's browser test.
- **The player's app asks which floor the player is on, and draws the field plan with the positions they may see** (ML-5's floor picker and MF-14/MF-40's "the player's view still does not draw the plan", built 2026-10-27; decisions PM-1 to PM-9; no wire change, no controller change; in software only, not yet on a phone at a field): on a field with levels, a row of buttons under *Share my position* (the levels' names and *Not sure*) sets the floor once; the GPS reports carry `level` (`null` for *Not sure*; none until the player says anything, so the controller keeps the level it had), a room beacon's own level wins and a beacon report never carries one (`app/lib/position/position_reporter.dart`, `app/lib/player/player_screens.dart`). A *Field map* card (`app/lib/player/field_map.dart`, `app/lib/position/field_plan.dart`) draws the level's plan image (`GET /field/levels/{n}/image`) with the features the controller sends this token (pins, circles, areas, the boundary outline with the outside dimmed) and the positions of the stream, placed by a beacon's plan place or by the level's map position, a floor selector, hollow dots for a player with no floor, a legend and a screen-reader label for every pin and dot. Vector plan only: the app has no tile layer. `FieldResponse` now carries the bytes of an image answer. Tests: `app/test/position/floor_and_map_test.dart` and `beacon_scanner_test.dart` (phone widths 320 and 360 px). Manuals (en, pt) and the app and phase 3 docs updated; the zh-TW texts use only characters the test font already has.
- **A field controller takes the field's check-in settings from the site server, and branding changes reach it on the stream** (decisions BR24-1 to BR24-6, closing the items BR24 and S18-11 left for later; in software, the Pi, the phone controller and the site server, the same behaviour on both controllers): `GET /api/v1/sync/field?settings=1` also carries the field's `max_replicas`, federation label and limit template (whole), and the controller with `[sync] branding = "site"` makes its check-in match, keeping the template in its store; the site server sends a `field.changed` note on the stream whenever the field's branding, saved teams (also a club's name or crest) or settings change, and the controller pulls at once instead of at its next cycle (it opens the stream for that alone, sending nothing of the game). A standby controller does all of this with its own `[sync]`. Additive: without the query the answer is byte for byte the old one, older controllers ignore the note, older servers send neither; `protocol/sync.md`, `protocol/vectors/sync.json` (`answers` 15 valid and 67 invalid, `stream` 19 valid and 51 invalid), the game master's manual (section 12).
- **Staff pages for the APIs that had none, and the remote GM's view pushed to the browser** (Phase 4 follow-up FU9, decisions FU9-1 to FU9-7, S19-11 built; `server/atalaia_server/web/`, `server/atalaia_server/api/remote_gm.py`): pages for a field's roles and a game's roles, sync (API) keys (labelled, at most two live, key shown once then its fingerprint), branding and saved teams, platform links, the clubs and a club, the firmware catalogue and server administration, by the FU2 rules (no rule in the page, four languages, `no-store`, `noindex`, CSP unchanged, bearer token, 375 px); the remote GM page now reads `GET /api/v1/fields/{field}/remote-gm/stream` (server-sent events via `fetch`, `view` events at most two a second, new stream every five minutes) instead of polling every 5 s, which stays as the fallback. Tests in `server/tests/test_staff_pages.py`, `test_remote_gm_push.py` and `server/tests_browser/`.
- **The app shows the player's own game history, and reconnects to an I/O pod by itself** (decision S20-8, the history views in the app; PF15, the pod's link; `app/lib/site/history_screens.dart`, `app/lib/pod/pod_reconnect.dart`, `app/README.md`, `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/player.md`): on the site screen, **Game history** (after the login) lists the games the account played, newest first, from the site server's existing player history API (no server change), and a game shows its scores, the winner and the player's own events in words; nothing is kept on the phone. When the link to an I/O pod breaks, the app opens the same pod again with growing waits (1, 2, 4, 8, 16 then 30 s) while the pod screen or the game is open, checks it is the same pod (another pod answering is refused and the link stays down) before it takes it back, shows the state (linked, trying again with the attempt number, refused, unlinked) and stops when the person unlinks the pod (**Unlink** on the pod's screen, **Unlink the I/O pod** in the game's admin menu). During a game the radio through the pod is configured again with a new epoch after a reconnect. Texts in pt-PT, en, de and zh-TW; tests in `app/test/site/history_screens_test.dart`, `app/test/pod/pod_reconnect_test.dart` and `app/test/prop/prop_pod_reconnect_test.dart`.
- **Hosted mode: per-field quotas, an export a field's administrator can download, and a deleted field's open sockets close at once** (S21-12's three gaps that need no real deployment, built 2026-10-27; decisions FU8-1 to FU8-8; `server/atalaia_server/quotas.py`, `field_export.py`, `api/export.py`, `tenant_watch.py`; no migration): `GET /api/v1/fields/{id}/export` gives the field's `admin` a ZIP (`atalaia-field-data` 1: manifest, README and one JSON Lines file per table) of the field's own data without credentials, e-mails or addresses, audit-logged with its SHA-256, one at a time and one per 5 minutes; accounts, scheduled games, registrations, synced games, live ATAK certificates and open controller streams are limited per field in hosted mode (`ATALAIA_QUOTA_*`, `409 quota_exceeded` with the quota and limit, streams closed 4429); the server now reads the registry every `ATALAIA_HOSTED_WATCH_S` seconds (2) while a socket is open and closes the controller streams, remote-GM links and ATAK connections of a field that was deleted (4404) or suspended (4403). Tests: `test_field_export.py`, `test_hosted_limits.py`, `test_config.py`.
- **Staff actions on registrations: paid, no-show, move, restore, the replicas, the import and the export, and the last roster pull** (the FU6 pages listed them as missing and the maintainer approved building them on 2026-10-27; Phase 4 follow-up FU7, decisions FU7-1 to FU7-9; migration `0014_registration_staff`; `server/atalaia_server/services/registration.py`, `api/registrations.py`, `web/`, `server/README.md`): the field's `admin` and `gm` mark a registration paid or unpaid (a note with who and when; the server processes no payment; the player sees that it is paid, not who), mark a registered player a no-show after the game and take back their own mark (the controller's check-in is never undone and its next push replaces the mark), move a registration to another published game of the field that has not started (the places, the federation rule and one registration per person hold; the ticket stays valid because it holds the registration, not the game; a team, a number or replicas the new game does not take are dropped and named), bring a withdrawn registration back before the start while a place is left (without a ticket: the player asks for a new one in the app), and edit the replicas on the page (an unchanged list changes nothing, an unchanged replica keeps its declaration id); the registrations page has the CSV export and the import (check the file, then import with the field's confirmation of its own basis). The scheduled game records when a controller last pulled its roster, with the API key's label and fingerprint and the User-Agent (a controller's GET carries no other identity), and the roster page shows it. New audit actions `registration_restore`, `registration_move`, `registration_paid`, `registration_no_show` (never a name); erasing a staff account unlinks its marks; `GET /api/v1/fields-by-slug/{slug}` also gives `replica_types`. Texts in pt-PT, English, German and Traditional Chinese; checked at 375 px. Tests: `server/tests/test_registration_staff.py`, `test_migrations.py`, `test_staff_pages.py`, `server/tests_browser/`.
- **Indoor BLE beacon positions** (ADR-P3-6's last unbuilt source, built 2026-10-27; decisions BLE-1 to BLE-9; in software and fakes, **not run with real beacons**): the beacon registry on both controllers (`controller/atalaia_controller/beacons.py`, store schema 14; the phone controller's `controller_field_beacons.dart`; `GET|POST /api/v1/beacons`, `PUT|DELETE /api/v1/beacons/{major:minor}`, the GM writes, staff read it all and everyone else only each beacon's `id` and `uuid`; shared cases in `controller_api.json`), a `ble` position report accepted only for a registered beacon and stored with its room, zone, level and plan place, the console's *Indoor beacons* panel with a round pin per placed beacon (`beacons-panel.js`), the app's Kotlin `BeaconScanPlugin` (low-power scan, a hardware filter per registered UUID, one window a game interval of 3 to 30 s, nothing kept), `beacon_scanner.dart` (the strongest registered beacon above -95 dBm) and the reporter's rule (a beacon heard in the last two intervals replaces the GPS fixes), the app's explanation and *Continue* / *Not now* before the Bluetooth and location prompts, ATAK showing an indoor player at the beacon's place with the room in the remarks, and the player's screen showing their room. The manifest's `BLUETOOTH_SCAN` no longer says `neverForLocation` (Android hides beacons from an app that says so), so the I/O pod's scan now asks for the location from Android 12 too; iOS is unchanged (GPS only: iBeacons are ranged through CoreLocation, a Swift plugin that needs a Mac); the manuals' player and game master chapters, the ADR's "As built" section and `decisions-to-review.md`.
- **Staff pages for scheduled games, registrations and the game-day roster** (found while writing the user manuals: the site server kept them but only its API reached them; Phase 4 follow-up FU6, decisions FU6-1 to FU6-7, 2026-10-27; `server/atalaia_server/web/`, `server/README.md`): `/staff/fields/<field>/schedule` lists the field's scheduled games (upcoming, past, all) and creates a draft, edits a draft or a published game, deletes a draft and, for the field's admin, publishes and cancels with a reason, with times typed and shown in the field's time zone; `.../schedule/<game>/registrations` lists who registered (display name, team asked for, federation number when the game asks for one, replicas, ticket, status) and lets the admin and the gm change a name, a team or a federation number and withdraw a registration until the game starts, as the API allows; `.../schedule/<game>/roster` shows the list the controller pulls (`GET /api/v1/sync/roster`) with a print style sheet, and says whether a controller game was made from the scheduled game, the walk-ins it reported and that the server does not record roster pulls. The pages call the API that already existed (no endpoint, column or migration added; the rules stay in `services/scheduling.py` and `services/registration.py`), hold no data and have the API's role rules, in pt-PT, English, German and Traditional Chinese, at a phone's width. Not built: marking a registration paid or checked in (the model has neither; arrivals come from the controller), the import and CSV export on a page, editing replicas. Tests: `tests/test_staff_schedule.py`, `tests/test_staff_pages.py`, `tests_browser/` `ScheduleTests` (Chromium, 375 px).
- **Replica QR labels: the app and the GM console make the sticker the marshal's queue scans** (found while writing the user manuals: the app registered replicas as `ATL-` and eight characters and the queue scanned that text, but nothing made the label; `app/lib/ui/replica_label.dart`, `ShareFilePlugin.kt`, `controller/console/src/labels.*`, `protocol/checkin.md` "Replica labels"): the QR holds the label text only, `ATL-` and the first eight characters of the replica's id, upper case, and nothing personal. App: **Show label** (a big QR with the code, the screen kept on and bright) and **Share / print label** (a 30 x 30 mm PNG at 300 dpi for one sticker, or A4 sheets of 54 labels with cutting guides, for a player with many replicas; drawn with `dart:ui` and the `qr` package already in the app, no new Dart dependency) on the player's replica list, the marshal's replica registry (QR-tagged replicas; **Share / print all labels** in its title bar) and the queue; the PNG goes to Android's share sheet, which lists printing, through a new small `ShareFilePlugin.kt` and a `FileProvider` limited to `cache/labels/` (iOS has no twin yet and says so). Console: the Check-in tab's replica rows have **Print labels** and the head **Print labels for everyone checked in**, which open `labels.html`, a printable page like the poster with one 30 x 30 mm label per declared replica, drawn client-side with the console's QR code (the player's name and the replica's kind show on the screen only). To make the label known before anyone chronographs the replica, a marshal's phone now registers a declared replica without a field id under its **declaration's id** instead of a random one (so a second marshal's phone makes the same label and a replica opened twice is no longer registered twice), the queue's scan matches a label to the declarations it lists even when this phone never registered the replica (before, a label of an unregistered declaration matched any unregistered declaration), and the player's app keeps the `replica_id` the field gives each declared replica, so a printed label stays good in later games. Texts in the app's four languages and the console's four (pt-PT "tu", gender-neutral); the zh-tw app texts need no new characters in the test font subset. Tests: `app/test/ui/replica_label_test.dart` (the PNG and the sheet read back pixel by pixel to the QR of the label text, the label text through the scanner's own `classifyCode` and `byTag`, the screens), `chrono_queue_test.dart`, and a console browser test that reads every label back with `zbarimg`. Manuals (player, marshal, game master; English and European Portuguese), `app/README.md`, `protocol/checkin.md`.
- **Field controllers pull other fields' chrono records of the players on their roster** (decision S14-5's pull, built 2026-10-27): the site server had the consent and the lookup, the controllers only pushed. New `GET /api/v1/sync/chrono?scheduled_game_id=` answers, for that scheduled game's roster only, what the staff's cross-field lookup already allows (both fields sharing, the person's live consent for each field, through the account that holds the id here), each record as signed with its field's name and the marshal's key, the newest 100 per player; the audit log has the counts (`chrono_shared_pull`), not the players. The Pi (`sync_client.py`, `sync_format.py`, `checkin.py`, `store.py` schema 13 `shared_readings`) and the phone (`controller_sync.dart`, `controller_sync_format.dart`, `controller_checkin.dart`) pull it with each roster until the game starts, verify every signature, drop what is off the roster, over 365 days old or already a local measurement, and keep the rest apart from their own readings: never judged, counted in the list's states or sent back, a local measurement never replaced, a consent withdrawn removes them at the next cycle, and they go with the readings' retention, the player's erasure and the player's chrono erasure. The chrono list and the check-in row carry `shared_readings` (`from_other_field`, the field's name) and the console shows them under *From other fields* (en, pt, de, zh-tw). `protocol/sync.md`, `protocol/vectors/sync.json` (kind `shared_chrono`: 2 valid and 14 invalid answers, and 2 `shared_chrono_apply` cases both controllers pass). Tests: the server's `test_chrono.py`, the Pi's `test_sync_schedule.py`, the phone's `controller_sync_test.dart` and `controller_sync_format_test.dart`. Decision S14-5's texts for review (the consent wording in the privacy notice templates) are unchanged and did not block it.
- **Players' NFC tags are signed by the field controller and written from the GM console, the phone controller's screen and the marshal's app; a marshal's phone no longer needs the field join key for them** (the first user manuals' review flagged the join key on every marshal's phone as a security smell; decisions TW-1 to TW-7, 2026-10-27; `protocol/nfc-tag.md`): `POST /api/v1/games/{id}/tags` (`controller/atalaia_controller/player_tags.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_tags.dart`; the GM token, or a marshal's for that game; the read token and another game's marshal get 403) takes `{"player", "team"?}` and answers the tag's payload, its NDEF record and its Type 2 memory in hex, signed with the live game's key, which never leaves the controller (400 for a bad id or team, 409 when the game is not live on the controller); one shared contract case ("player tags", `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`, with a new `tag_check` step that decodes the answer with the live game's key on each side) passes on both controllers. The console's Check-in tab has a *Players' tags* card (`components/tag-writer.js`: a checked-in player or an id, a team; Web NFC (`NDEFReader`) writes the record where the browser has it, otherwise the bytes are shown as hex with a file to download and the app that writes them; in pt-PT, English, German and Traditional Chinese); the phone controller's own screen has a *Players' tags* section that writes with its NFC (`controller_tags_screen.dart`, asking its own controller on the loopback address as the field card does); and the app's marshal role asks the field controller for each tag and writes the record it gets after checking it is the tag asked for (`app/lib/marshal/tag_writer.dart`), so **the marshal's tag writer is gone from the join key**: it no longer picks a game with a join card or code, and it needs the controller (the field's Wi-Fi) for every tag, offline included (decision TW-3). The phone controller's status carries the game's teams for it. New tests: Python contract and unit cases, a browser test, Dart widget tests (`app/test/marshal/tag_writer_test.dart`) and the contract runner's `tag_check`; docs: `protocol/nfc-tag.md`, `protocol/checkin.md`, both READMEs, the game master and marshal manuals in English and Portuguese, ADR-P3-9's amendment, and PD14 marked as amended.
- **A scenario the site archives and a preset it deletes are removed from the field controllers on their next sync** (S20-8's "removing a scenario or preset from controllers", built as decision S20-9, 2026-10-26): no wire change: the `presets` answer is the field's whole live library, so a controller removes what it installed from the site (`sync.site_library`) and the answer no longer lists, and an old controller keeps working. Kept until over: a scenario a game that is not ended runs or a scheduled game names, and one the GM replaced with their own manifest; a GM's own presets and scenarios are never touched. On the Pi (`sync_client.py`, `scenario_service.uninstall`, `ModeRegistry.remove`, the store's `delete_mode` and `delete_preset`) and the phone (`controller_sync.dart`, `ControllerModes.remove`/`deletePreset`, `ScenarioService.uninstall`); `sync.library` counts `scenarios_removed` and `presets_removed`; `protocol/sync.md` has the rule and `protocol/vectors/sync.json` the shared `library_removal` cases (both controllers pass them: `library_removals`). Tests: the vectors on both sides, the Pi's and the phone's client (archive and delete, restore, running game, scheduled game, the GM's own manifest) and the real server with a real controller (`server/tests/test_scenarios_api.py`). No server change, no migration.
- **The phone controller serves the field-map page with the Pi's own headers** (decision MF-64's first item, built 2026-10-26): `field-map.html` under `/console/` now gets the same Content-Security-Policy as the Pi's `FIELD_MAP_HEADERS` (OpenStreetMap's tiles, the two Overpass instances, Nominatim, each open orthophoto's WMS as images and connections and the WMTS origins as images) and `geolocation=(self)`, so *Load the field from a map* can reach its map services from a phone controller; every other console page and file keeps the strict same-origin policy. `console_files.dart` (`fieldMapSecurityHeaders`, `consoleHeadersFor`) and `controller_api.dart`; Dart tests for the headers per path and a check that the origins equal those in `api.py`. The Overpass `User-Agent`, a WMTS for the orthophoto layer and an instance setting stay left out (the Pi has none). Not run against a real phone.
- **The controller leaves a gap between its own frames** (decision SL-13's pacing half, built 2026-10-26; decision SL-39; RFC 0001 **Amendment 15**; no wire change, no box to update; `controller/atalaia_controller/engine.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_radio.dart`): the relay study found that 0.8 s between the controller's own frames adds 0-22 points to the setup CMDs that reach their box. Both controllers now queue every frame they send in a game (ACKs, TIME, CMDs; one FIFO, no priorities, 8 frames) and hand them to the modem one at a time, 800 ms after the previous one was on the air (the modem's TX_ACK says when; `[radio] controller_pace_ms` on the Pi, `ControllerConfig.controllerPaceMs` on the phone). **It ships off (0)**, because the model shows it costs some of the controller's ACKs (55.7 to 48.0 %, 83.3 to 68.2 % heard in some cells); 800 is the candidate for the 20-box field test (`docs/runbooks/radio-field-test.md` has the comparison), and the maintainer decides from it. The game's end drops the waiting CMDs and TIMEs (the ACKs stay) and sends its own CMD and TIME at once, the modem's listen-before-talk and duty-cycle guard are below it and unchanged, and `GET /api/v1/controller` shows `tx_queue`. Tests: `controller/tests/test_tx_pacing.py` and `app/test/controller/tx_pacing_test.dart` (a fake modem and clock: spacing, order, TX_ACK, queue limit, flush, seq wrap), two shared cases "TX pacing" in `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json` (a case sets `controller_pace_ms`; the older cases run with 0). The study's `H_paced` rows re-measured on the built parameters (table 7 of `docs/review/relay-study-results.md`, with a new table of the pacing's cost): the same 0-22 points, no frame dropped by the queue. **Not built, still open:** a CMD box ACK (a wire change, for the maintainer) and a scenario-level re-send; the CMD stays one shot.
- **GM console: the remote GM's log, "the poster changed", a pausable countdown, the scoreboard's QR code, and wording fixes** (decisions S19-11, check-R8, check-R15 and PD13 built; `controller/console/src/`, both controllers serve it): the Controller tab's *Remote GM log* (`components/remote-gm-log.js`, from `GET /api/v1/remote-gm`); the console says when the poster's addresses or the field's certificate differ from the poster last printed (`POST /api/v1/checkin/poster/printed`, `printed` and `changed` on `GET /api/v1/checkin/poster`, shared cases `poster_change` in `protocol/vectors/checkin.json`, on the Pi and the phone controller alike) with an alert and a notice in the Check-in tab; the 10 s "Print the poster?" countdown stays the default but waits for the pointer and keyboard focus and has a pause button (WCAG 2.2.1); the Game tab's *Share the scoreboard with players* QR code, `http://<host>:<port>/scoreboard#token=<read code>` as the app's *Follow a game* takes it (`GET /api/v1/scoreboard-link`, GM token). The log has words for every event the built-in modes emit, in the four console languages (the app's wording where it has it). Portuguese: the poster says «Participante» as the app does, "tu" and gender-neutral wording throughout (the formal forms, `jogadores`, `red, blue` and `«Ready»` fixed, `código de acesso` as the login says), no decision or ADR id in any console text, and the site says "servidor do site" as the glossary does.
- **A way back into the Field Box's Wi-Fi setup** (found while writing the user manuals, 2026-10-26; decisions WS-1, WS-2; `firmware/esp32/main/preset_ui.c`, `shell.c`, `admin_pin.c`): `BRING_UP.md` said to hold reset or to choose "Setup" in the admin menu, and neither existed (the portal opened only on a box never set up; `config_store_reset` had no caller). Now the game picker's list ends with **Wi-Fi setup** after the games, behind the admin PIN, in the box's language (the OLED too): `#` asks *SET UP AGAIN?*, `#` confirms, `*` goes back; or `@setup <PIN>` on the USB console (`@setup` alone while no admin PIN is set; the PIN is counted and made to wait like any other). Either clears the box's "configured" flag and restarts it into the portal; the field join key, the adoption and the stored games are kept, and neither works during a game or on a bench build, which has no portal. Host tests: the picker's states, pages and screens, `@setup`'s PIN rules, six new screen reference images and four OLED ones.
- **The setup portal is a captive portal, and its page speaks the box's languages** (found while writing the user manuals; decisions WS-3, WS-4): the box's screen said *Phones usually open it by themselves*, but there was no DNS and no redirect, so a phone showed nothing until its owner typed 192.168.4.1. Now the DHCP server offers the box as the DNS server, a small DNS responder on the SoftAP's address answers every name with the box's (`wifi_setup_dns_reply`: A and ANY get the address, every other type an empty answer, malformed packets none; fuzzed in the host tests), and every HTTP address but the page and its form (Android's `/generate_204`, Apple's `/hotspot-detect.html`, Windows' `/connecttest.txt`, ...) redirects to the page. The page, which was English only, is in English, Portuguese, German or Traditional Chinese: the box's language when a game gave it one, else the browser's `Accept-Language`, `/?lang=xx` (links at its foot) to choose; the reasons a form is refused and *Saved* are in the same language. Flash: the Field Box image grew by 12 064 bytes (1 931 168 to 1 943 232 of the 2 097 152 of its partition; 7 % free). The German and Chinese wait for a native-speaking player like the rest.
- **AS923-TW's CAD detection peak is 29, not 23** (decision SL-38, measured 2026-10-26 with SF9/500 kHz on 868.3 MHz): Semtech's 23 found a quiet channel busy in 17.8 % of probes; 29 gave 0.5 % and still detected frames. The core's region table, its tests and the docs say 29; it stays unproven until the gate runs on an AS923-TW image. The bench's `@lbt` log queue moved to PSRAM (35 KB did not fit in internal RAM, so the first proxy run refused to start).
- **`reset-totp`, the recovery for an administrator who lost their authenticator** (decision S11-7, which had left it for later and recovered the account by a database edit; `server/atalaia_server/cli.py`): `python -m atalaia_server reset-totp --username NAME --reason TEXT [--yes] [--field SLUG]` clears the account's TOTP secret, enrolment and last-used step, revokes every session of the account (access tokens stop working at once; a login half-way through the TOTP step is refused), and writes a `totp_reset` row to the server's audit log (the account, the operator's system user, the reason, the sessions revoked, `via: cli`). The password is untouched; at the next login the account has a session that has not passed TOTP and enrols a new secret. It refuses an unknown account or one with no TOTP, asks for the username to be typed again unless `--yes`, and takes `--field` in hosted mode like `create-admin`. The server keeps no recovery codes or remembered devices, so none are cleared. Tests in `server/tests/test_roles.py` (PostgreSQL); documented in `server/README.md` and the preview runbook.
- **The app's secrets at rest are sealed with the Android Keystore** (decision PD6's follow-up "before a public release", built 2026-10-26; Android only, iOS unchanged): the field join key, the marshal's Ed25519 seed and the player's read code are no longer in `settings.json`. `KeystorePlugin.kt` (channel `org.atalaia.app/keystore`, registered in `MainActivity` like the other plugins) makes one AES-256-GCM key in the AndroidKeyStore (non-exportable; no user-authentication or unlocked-screen requirement, so the foreground service and a start after a reboot still work) and seals and opens bytes (nonce, ciphertext and tag, with a fixed AAD); `FileAppStore` keeps them in `secrets.enc` and **migrates an older plaintext `settings.json` on the first start** (seal, read back, rewrite without the secrets). Failing safe: a lost key (data restored on another phone) or a damaged file reads as "secrets missing, join again", the file is removed, nothing throws; a Keystore that did not answer keeps the file for a later start; if sealing fails nothing goes to disk in the clear. Where the plugin does not exist (iOS, tests) behaviour is as before. Not yet sealed: `player_secret`, the site refresh token, the controller's site API key. Tests: `app/test/storage/secret_vault_test.dart` (fake vault, mocked channel), `app/integration_test/keystore_test.dart` (real Keystore on the CI emulator; not run locally). `app/README.md` and the PD6 row of `docs/review/decisions-to-review.md` updated.
- **The simulator's web view takes the inputs a mode's role uses, and its event log downloads** (HANDOVER's "Web view extras"; `simulator/atalaia_sim/live.py`, `web.py`, `web/`): each node offers, besides press and hold, a tag read (player, team, and a valid, forged, other-game or blank tag, checked by the core as on a box), a keypad (keys or a typed code), a hit (strength 0 to 255) and GM commands (`set_owner` and the mode's own commands), only where the role's script handles them, so Codes, Bomb, Medic, Kill Confirmed, Respawn, Hacking Terminal, Money Factory and Target Windows can be played from the page and from the mode editor's "Test in the simulator"; the *Events* panel downloads the log as JSON or CSV (`/api/log.json`, `/api/log.csv`; CSV cells that start like a formula are quoted). New actions `scan`, `keys`, `hit` and `command` take the same limits as the others. Tests in `simulator/tests/test_live.py` and the editor's browser test.
- **The GM's owner override works in Domination, and ATAK marks Progressive Domination's active box** (decisions E7 and PD-MODE-16, both open items closed 2026-10-26): Domination handles `cmd.set_owner` as Hold-All and Last Stand do (`modes/domination/node.lua`, test `gm_override.yaml`; Bomb is left out, its bomb has no owner), and the boxes' ATAK markers say `active` in their remarks while a mode has made the box active (`controller/atalaia_controller/atak.py` and `app/lib/controller/controller_atak.dart` identically; shared known answers `node_remarks` in `protocol/vectors/atak_xml.json`; tests on both sides). `modes/README.md` lists the modes that handle the override.
- **The AS923-TW listen-before-talk CAD test on a legal EU channel** (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-26: prove detPeak 23 for SF9/500 kHz on the 868 MHz bench boards in Portugal, where 921.5 MHz is not allowed; decision SL-38; bench image only, the EU868 image and every normal image unchanged): `@lbt tx|probe [frames] proxy=as923 [peak=N] [min=N] [power=dBm]` runs AS923-TW's SF9, 500 kHz and its CAD settings from the core's table at **868.3 MHz (868.05-868.55 MHz, inside sub-band g: 1 %, 25 mW ERP)**, with the HAL's new `set_profile` (`hal_radio_ops_t`, `radio_limits_set_modulation`) putting the build's modulation, channel and limits back when the run ends; `radio/lbt_profile.c` builds the profile and asserts the channel is wholly inside g and clear of 920-925 MHz; -9 dBm conducted by default; the duty guard stays at 70 % of g's 36 s, so `tx` is capped at 123 frames (100 default, about 8.2 s of air, 0.23 % of the hour). `LBT,cfg` now states profile, modulation, frequency, SF, bandwidth, the CAD settings, whether detPeak is proven, the duty limit and the power; `tools/bench_lbt_gate.py` reports them, points at a detPeak sweep on a FAIL and refuses (exit 66) a proxy log outside 868.0-868.6 MHz or on AS923. Runbook section "The AS923-TW modulation on 868.3 MHz" with the exact commands for both boards. 6 new host test functions in `test_lbt.c`, new self-tests in the gate tool. Not run on a board yet.
- **The first user manuals** (`docs/manuals/`, in English and European Portuguese; the maintainer's request of 2026-10-26): a player manual, a game master and organiser manual, a marshal manual and a Field Box operator manual, each with its troubleshooting section, a one-page quick start per language and an index. They follow the screens and steps the software shows today (the app's strings, the GM console, the site server's staff pages, the box's own screens), mark what depends on hardware as not yet tested on a field, reuse the project page's pictures and link its glossary. The project page links them from its "Try it today" section and its footer in all four languages (German and Traditional Chinese lead to the English index; their own manuals are out of scope for now).
- **Listen before talk in the boxes' transmit path** (the maintainer approved building decision SL-25, 2026-10-26, after its bench gate passed; RFC 0001 **Amendment 14**; decisions SL-30 to SL-37; SL-25 is marked built and SL-19 superseded; the wire format is unchanged and nothing was flashed): before every transmission the radio task asks `hal_radio_channel_busy()` (the receiver's preamble and header flags first, which cost nothing and cover the whole frame being received, then one CAD) and, if the channel is busy, holds the frame in its one transmit slot and tries again after the core's random 20-200 ms, without blocking the task; after 8 busy answers in a row the frame is sent anyway (as the study's model did). **Core**: `atalaia_lbt_*` (`core/src/lbt.c`: the tries and the wait from the box's own entropy; 7 host tests with a seeded stub) and the CAD's settings in the region table (`atalaia_region_info()->cad`: 2 symbols, detection minimum 10, peak 22 at SF7/125 kHz and 23 at SF9/500 kHz; the AS923-TW peak is Semtech's figure and **unproven** until the bench gate has run on an AS923-TW image). **Firmware**: the radio task's transmit step is now plain C, `radio_txs_*` (`main/radio/radio_lbt.[ch]`), tested on a scripted fake HAL (busy N times then free, busy for ever, a receiver flag instead of a CAD, the duty-cycle budget, order, tags) and run by the host box end to end (`controller/tests/test_box_e2e.py`, `box_host --no-lbt` and `lbtbusy N`); the loop turns every ~10 ms with it (new HAL op `set_recv_wait_ms`; it was 50 + 10 ms), unchanged without it. **The duty-cycle budget is unchanged**: a CAD is not time on air, the limit is spent at the real send, after the wait, and a box whose budget is empty does not listen. **Config**: Kconfig `ATALAIA_LBT`, on by default, in the Field Box, the modem and the pod (the study's model has the controller listen too); `python3 tools/docker_fw.py build --lbt=off` for the field test that compares the two; Kconfig only, because a field-level switch would be a new join key (decision SL-33). **Counters**: `lbt_cads`, `lbt_deferrals`, `lbt_gave_up` in `field_link_stats_t`, the `radio_stats` line (box and modem; `RADIO_STATS_LINE_MAX` 544) and `tools/bench_radio_log.py`. **Re-measured on the hardware's own timing** (results section 13: an 8 ms CAD, the receiver's flags first, the loop 11 ms with LBT and 60 ms without, the tries and the wait by the core's own code through ctypes; 432 runs): the gain holds, a little smaller than the ideal CAD's (EU868, 20 boxes in a mesh: STATEs heard 64.6 > 89.1 %, capture latency p95 3.4 > 1.4 s, airtime +0.3 point, no frame refused in any run; 30 boxes: 51.1 > 77.1 %), about 1 050 CADs a box-hour (0.9-1.4 % of the TX energy at the measured 8 ms), and **frames sent after 8 waits are common in a crowded field** (84 a box-hour at 20 boxes in a mesh, 254 at 30). Docs: RFC 0001 Amendment 14, the firmware README, `docs/runbooks/radio-field-test.md` (how to run the 20-box test with LBT on and off).
- **The Field Box's pin table and wiring diagrams on the project page's build page** (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-26: "put also the pinning diagrams we already have"; `site/src/build.html`, `tools/build_site.py`): the Field Box section gets its GPIO table (checked against Kconfig like the others) and the two WireViz drawings, copied from `hardware/field-box/wiring/` into `site/img/wiring/` at build time with `--check`, shown in pt, en, de and zh-tw with translated captions and alt text (the drawings' own labels stay English: pin names and part numbers), on a white sheet in dark mode and opening full size on tap; the Satellite and the I/O pod point at them.
- **The radio report for the field test: a once-a-minute `radio_stats` line on the box's serial port, and a bench tool** (decision SL-18, the cheap field test; first version for the bench, 2026-10-26; `firmware/esp32/main/radio/radio_stats.[ch]`, `box_game.c`, `shell.c`, `modem/modem_task.c`, `tools/bench_radio_log.py`, `docs/runbooks/radio-field-test.md`): while a game is on a Field Box (and once more when it ends) it logs one JSON line under the tag `radio_stats` with `field_link_stats_t` (events sent, retries, acked, expired, relayed, relay_skipped, relay_cancelled, relay_no_slot, duplicates, tx_refused, and rx, rx_rejected, states, hellos), `duty_remaining_ms`, the neighbour count, the game, the node and the uptime; the USB modem logs its own (rx, tx, tx_fail, field frames, duty budget) on its UART0 console, since its USB port carries only frames. Read-only: no change to what the radio sends, no heap, a stack buffer. `tools/bench_radio_log.py` takes a log file or a live port (opened without raising DTR/RTS) and prints a per-minute table, a summary (retries per event, % acked, the duty budget's slope and minutes until empty, refusals) and the study's row for the field size (the study has none below 5 boxes: for one box it says what to compare with). Host tests: the line's format byte for byte (`test_radio_stats.c`), its timing in a game (`test_box_game.c`), the tool on the same line (`controller/tests/test_bench_radio_log.py`). Docs: SL-18's row, the runbook (bench, then 5 and 10 boxes), the firmware README.
- **The listen-before-talk bench gate, built, run pending** (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-26: SL-25's "a CAD sees only a preamble; the gain holds only if the busy test also covers a frame the receiver is already receiving; above 90 % across the whole frame, or the answer is don't"; decisions SL-26 to SL-29; **no change to normal behaviour**: the test is in the bench image only and does nothing until a console command is typed): the radio HAL's `channel_busy()` (`hal_radio_ops_t`, SX1262 and stub) asks the receiver first (PreambleDetected or HeaderValid in `GetIrqStatus`: busy, with no CAD, which would lose the frame it is receiving; RxDone pending: not busy, untouched), then does one CAD, then receives again, and reports when it looked, the CAD's duration and the re-arm's (`radio/channel_busy.c`, plain C and host-tested); `hal_radio_ops_t.last_rx_done_us` gives the DIO1 edge's own timestamp. Bench image (`ATALAIA_LBT_BENCH`, in `sdkconfig.bench`): `@lbt tx [frames]` sends STATE-length frames (48-56 bytes) at random 300-700 ms gaps on the field channel, stopping at 1000 frames or when the sub-band's budget left falls under 70 %; `@lbt probe [frames [gap_min_us gap_max_us]]` probes every 2-5 ms, receives the frames, rebuilds each one's window from its RxDone time and airtime, classifies every probe (hit or miss by tenth of the frame and by preamble / header / payload; false busy between frames) and prints machine-readable `LBT,` lines and a summary every 25 frames (`radio/lbt_bench.c`, `lbt_stats.c`; the test runs in the radio task in place of its loop). `python3 tools/bench_lbt_gate.py LOG` (or `--capture COM3 --send "@lbt probe 300"`) prints the table by tenth, the false-busy rate, the CAD time against SL-25's 4 ms, and PASS or FAIL against 90 %; its `--selftest` is in `check.sh` and CI. Runbook: `docs/runbooks/lbt-bench-gate.md` (flash both boards, run, put board 2 back to the modem image). 13 new host test functions (`test_lbt.c`). Not run on a board yet.
- **Listen-before-talk measured on the built relay rules** (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-26; decision **SL-25**, supersedes the numbers of SL-19; simulation only, nothing in `core/`, `firmware/` or the wire format changed): the relay study's harness gained `A_backoff_lbt` and `A_backoff_core_lbt` (A+backoff, in the model and on the core's own rules, with a CAD before every transmission, a simulator-side wrapper because the core has no CAD), a CAD counter and `python -m atalaia_sim.relay_report --lbt-json` (tables with the CAD's energy next to the TX energy). 432 runs (10/20/30 boxes, three layouts, EU868 and AS923-TW, Domination and the scenario, three seeds, 90 minutes), results section 11: STATEs heard 52-80 % > 81-99 % in a mesh, capture latency p95 down 1.6-7 times in Domination, CQB of 30 ACKed 91 > 99 %, airtime +0.0 to +0.6 point, no frame refused, the CAD costs 0.7-2.4 % of the TX energy. Recommendation: adopt behind a config key after a bench check that the radio's busy test covers the whole frame (a CAD alone sees only the preamble); the firmware work (HAL CAD before TX, the CAD parameters per region, the radio task's pending slot, tests) is estimated at 4-5 days. Tests in `simulator/tests/test_relay_study.py`.
- **The relay and retry rules for a field of 20-30 boxes, built** (the maintainer accepted decision SL-16, 2026-10-26; RFC 0001 **Amendment 13**; decisions SL-21 to SL-24; the draft `docs/rfcs/0000-relay-ack-and-retry-rules.md` is marked superseded): the study's "A+backoff", as the study specified and measured it, in the core (`atalaia_relay_*` in `core/src/flood.c`, `atalaia_retry_gap_ms` and `atalaia_ack_covers` in `core/src/ack.c`) and the box's field link (`firmware/esp32/main/radio/field_link.c`): an EVENT or HELLO is resent after 3, 6, 12, 24 s and then every 30 s (each x 0.75-1.25), not every 3 s; a frame that asks for an ACK is relayed after 350-700 ms and not at all if the controller's ACK is heard first; the controller's ACK is relayed only for a box whose frame this box relayed in the last 30 s; every other frame is relayed with probability min(1, 3 / boxes heard in 10 minutes) after a random 20-600 ms; 8 relay slots instead of 4. No new message or field (the 15 s STATE stays, SL-5; the draft's optional key 19 `boxes` is not built); the delays and draws come from the box's own entropy (`shell.random`), the neighbour count from frames already heard; a mixed field is safe. RFC 0001 section 8 said a packet over the duty-cycle budget "is queued for retry"; the radio task drops it, and the study's numbers argue for keeping that (no box was refused a frame at 20-30 boxes with these rules): the sentence is corrected, not the code (SL-21). Tests: core (`test_flood.c`: slot count, delay ranges, ACK-overheard cancel, path gate, probability with a seeded entropy stub, neighbour expiry and eviction, the backoff schedule), the box's link (`test_field_link.c`: the same on the whole link with a scripted entropy source), and the relay study's harness re-run **with every box running the core's own rules** (`A_backoff_core`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/relay_core.py`, `simulator/tests/test_relay_core.py`): airtime, captures delivered and ACKed agree with the model within a point or two at 20 and 30 boxes on EU868 and AS923-TW (`docs/review/relay-study-results.md`, section "Re-measured on the built rules"). Not on air yet; listen-before-talk (SL-19) stays a separate, open decision.
- **Decisions accepted by the maintainer, and the buzz for Progressive Domination** (2026-10-26: "for the open questions, go with your recommendations"; `docs/review/decisions-to-review.md`: RG-1, PD-MODE-*, P5H-21, P5K-16, GIF-5 (stay on GIF), SL-9 and SL-13 (recommendations recorded, follow-ups listed, nothing built), RT-1 (a marshal may not register an Atalaia code as a replica's tag), and the open rows of SQ, VB, MR, PV, MF-82 to MF-93, iOS (NFC stays open), PE24-118 to PE24-129, CL-2, check-R and RF-AS923; PD-MODE-21 built: `progressive.activated` buzzes both teams, `progressive.completed` buzzes as "captured" for the player's team and "lost" for the other, `app/lib/player/field_buzz.dart`, tests in `app/test/player/field_buzz_test.dart`, item 11 of `app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md`).
- **The registry has a page, and the project page links to it** (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-26, after creating `github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-modes`; decision REG-PAGE-1: one static page per language, not a switcher): GitHub Pages served `index.json` at the registry's root, so a visitor saw JSON. `tools/registry_page.py` now builds `index.html` (English), `pt/`, `de/` and `zh-tw/` from `index.json` when the registry is published (`registry_check.py site`, so the template's workflow needs no change), with the texts in `registry/template/site/page.yaml`: what the registry is, its modes (name, author, version, verified badge, licence, download and signature links; "no modes yet, be the first" when empty), how to submit, what "verified" means and how a field controller uses `https://vakaobr.github.io/atalaia-modes/index.json`, in the project page's colours (light and dark, no script). `index.json` keeps its address and content type. Everything the registry supplies is escaped and only relative or `https://` addresses are linked (`registry/tests/test_registry_page.py`, with hostile names, authors and addresses; the template test also checks the pages are published). The project page's menu gains "Registry" (*Registo*, *Registry*, *模式登錄庫*, as `docs/translations/glossary.md` has it), the community and modes sections mention it, and the phase-5 roadmap bullets say the registry's repository exists and the project's registry key is the maintainer's task. `controller.example.toml` and `docs/community/registry.md` name the published index; step 6 of "Creating the repository" is done (steps 1 to 3 and 5 on 2026-10-26, step 4 pending).
- **A box's firmware update through the I/O pod** (the phase 3 roadmap's last pod gap, 2026-10-26; decisions PE24-130 to PE24-135, open; PE24-130 for the maintainer; in software and host tests, not yet on a pod with a phone): the roadmap said the pod did not relay the radio messages of a box's update, but the update has no frame of its own: the offer is CMD update (opcode 9), an ordinary game packet the phone sends as RADIO_TX, and the box's HELLO and `node.update_failed` come back as RADIO_RX, all of which every pod relays; the image and the report go over the field's Wi-Fi. So **no wire change and no IDENT key** (an older pod carries it as well as a new one; the capability proxy's file says so). What was built: `test_update_relay` in `firmware/esp32/test/test_pod.c` (the offer opened with the game key, the duty-cycle refusal and success back as RADIO_TX_ACK, the box's failure back as RADIO_RX, no radio chip and no game refused); the Android engine's end-to-end update test now also runs through the host pod (`boxes_e2e_test.dart`); and on the phone, a radio that cannot carry the offer (no radio, a pod with no radio chip, a link that is down) is **said in words** (`radio_problem` in the install answer, `radio` in `GET /api/v1/firmware`, in the console in four languages) and an offer the radio takes and then refuses (its duty-cycle budget, a full queue) **fails the update at once** (`radio_duty_cycle`, `radio_queue_full`) instead of staying `sent` until the 10-minute timeout, with either radio (`app/test/controller/firmware_radio_test.dart`, 6 tests). The USB modem path is unchanged; the Pi controller does not yet follow a refused offer (follow-up). `protocol/capability-proxy.md`, `protocol/firmware-update.md`, the app's and the firmware's READMEs and the project page's roadmap (four languages) updated.

- **The poster lists only addresses the controller has now** (the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-26, check-R8; decisions check-R9 to check-R13; `controller/atalaia_controller/addresses.py`, `app/lib/controller/field_addresses.dart`, the cases `poster_addresses` in `protocol/vectors/checkin.json`): a configured address that is on no interface (a stale `tls_names` or `[checkin] urls` entry, or an address in a saved poster) is left out of the check-in poster and of the server certificate and warned about in the log at every start; the saved poster is re-derived, keeping its Wi-Fi name, game and field name; a host name stays; a container keeps what is configured. Identical on the Pi and the phone controller (12 shared cases; the phone also re-derives when its addresses change, and renews its certificate when a name saved on the poster is not in it). `protocol/checkin.md` and the controller README say it.
- **"Print the check-in poster?" after a game is created** (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-26; decisions check-R14 to check-R19; `controller/console/src/components/poster-prompt.js`): a dialog that closes itself after 10 s with the count in sight, or sooner with Close or Esc, with a button to the poster page; focus moves in and is given back, Tab stays inside, the countdown is a polite live region, and a GM who is typing keeps the focus; "Don't show this again" is kept in the browser (Settings brings it back); not for a read token, in a phone's web view or where there is no poster to print; pt-PT, en, de, zh-TW; 6 browser tests.
- **The field's addresses raced, remembered and explained** (the maintainer's iPhone against the bench, 2026-10-26: a poster listed two dead DHCP addresses behind the live one; decisions check-R1 to check-R9, open; check-R8 for the maintainer; app only, the controllers unchanged): the check-in client, the first scan's CA fetch and the player feed try **all** the poster's addresses in a happy-eyeballs race (the next after 250 ms or at a failure, the first TLS handshake with the field's CA wins, the rest are cancelled; the feed no longer uses `urls.first`, reconnects included) with the pinning as strict as before; the winner is remembered per field, in memory and in the pinned field's record (`last_url`), and tried first; the last failure of each address (timeout, refused, no route, certificate not accepted, HTTP status) with its address and time is kept per field, written to the debug log and shown as a "Details" line under the screens that say the field controller does not answer (pt-PT, en, de, zh-tw). The report on how the controllers fill the poster's addresses (the Pi's and the phone's re-derive only the default; a saved poster, `[checkin] urls` and `tls_names` stay as configured) and a proposal are in the decisions. `app/lib/checkin/field_route.dart`, `failure_details.dart`; tests `app/test/checkin/field_route_test.dart` (fake attempts and real TLS sockets), `failure_details_test.dart`, `app/test/player/field_feed_routes_test.dart`.
- **The Dart suite on a Mac** (the maintainer's Mac session, 2026-10-26, which ran it at `f078f74`: 1783 passed, 140 failed, all environmental): the four golden files are tagged `golden` (`app/dart_test.yaml`), so a Mac runs `flutter test --exclude-tags golden` while Linux, `docker_app.py` and CI still run them; the tests that need the firmware host programs (`ATALAIA_POD_HOST`, `ATALAIA_MODEM_HOST`, `ATALAIA_BOX_HOST`) skip with a reason on a machine that is not Linux or with `ATALAIA_ALLOW_MISSING_HOSTS=1`, and still fail on Linux, in `docker_app.py` and in CI (`app/tool/check.sh` sets `ATALAIA_ALLOW_MISSING_HOSTS=0`); `boxes_e2e_test.dart` and `pod_controller_test.dart` no longer add a `LateInitializationError` to a failed setup; the host pod, modem and box build on macOS (`MSG_NOSIGNAL` fallback, no `-Werror` on Apple's Clang, mbedtls asked of the core), not yet tried on a Mac; `app/README.md` ("Tests on a Mac") and `app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md` (iPhone integration tests: `--no-uninstall`, allow Local Network and Location once by hand).
- **The iPhone checklist as an on-device test** (the maintainer's Mac session, 2026-10-26; `app/integration_test/ios_checklist_test.dart`, run with `flutter test integration_test/ios_checklist_test.dart -d <udid>` and optional `--dart-define`s `ATALAIA_TEST_CONTROLLER`, `ATALAIA_TEST_READ_TOKEN` and `ATALAIA_TEST_GM_TOKEN`; not yet run on an iPhone): the start language and the four languages without a layout overflow (no golden images), the iOS roles, the settings surviving a restart through the app's private folder, the version, the QR scanner and position sharing offered as unavailable, the GM console loading in the web view, the marshal list (a player not on it refused, one the GM listed accepted, the test adding and removing the marshal through the GM API itself), the buzzes on key events through a recording device and a stand-in stream, and a player's feed from a real controller; a check that needs a `--dart-define` it was not given is skipped with its reason, the iOS-only ones are skipped on Android, `integration_test/support.dart` knows iOS has no core, and `app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md` has a section "Integration tests on the iPhone".
- **The iOS side of the QR scanner, the `atalaia://` links and position sharing** (the maintainer's iPhone 15 Pro Max on iOS 27.2 with a free Apple ID, 2026-10-26; decisions iOS-13 to iOS-16, open; in Swift written without a Mac, compiled by CI's `ios-build` and the Mac session, not yet run on a device): `app/ios/Runner/QrScanPlugin.swift` (AVFoundation: a full-screen scanner with the hint and a cancel button, `AVCaptureMetadataOutput` with `.qr`; the same `org.atalaia.app/qr` contract as Android's, so a cancel, a refused camera and the simulator's missing camera read as nothing scanned and the typed join stays), `LinkPlugin.swift` (`CFBundleURLTypes` with `atalaia`, Flutter's scene life-cycle delegate: the link that started the app and the ones while it runs, on Android's `org.atalaia.app/links` channel; testable with `xcrun simctl openurl`) and `LocationPlugin.swift` (CoreLocation, **when in use only**, no background mode and no entitlement: Android's `location` channels, the permission through the device channel's `requestPermission`, one fix per interval, a one-time request for the precise location when only the approximate one was given), each registered from `AppDelegate.swift` and added to `project.pbxproj`. The player screen says on iOS that the position is shared only while the app is on the screen, and how to allow the location in iOS's Settings (four languages). `app/test/platform/ios_channels_test.dart` pins the channels' contract and the iOS-only texts. The iOS guide, `app/README.md`, the decisions and the project page's phase 3 line (pt, en, de, zh-tw) say what now works and how to test it on the simulator and the phone.
- **The remote GM may start a game anyway** (found by `server/tests/test_remote_gm_e2e.py` after the start guard, RJ-1: a remote GM could not start a game with a role that has no joined box; decisions RG-1 to RG-6 reverse RJ-4, open; RG-1 for the maintainer): the remote `start` takes an optional `{"force": true}` as the console's *Start anyway*; a start refused for missing roles comes back as `conflict` with the roles in a new optional `missing` of `command.result`; the site server's remote GM page asks *Start anyway?* (pt-PT, English, German, Traditional Chinese) and sends it again with `force`; a forced start is in the controller's `remote_commands` and the site server's audit log with `force`. Pi controller, phone controller, `protocol/vectors/sync.json`, `protocol/sync.md`, the site server and its tests (unit, end to end, Chromium).
