- Field and team branding (maintainer request, decisions BR1-BR24): a field or club running its own controller sets its name (the check-in poster's), a logo, an optional wallpaper and an accent colour in the GM console's *Settings* (GM token), kept by the controller across restarts and readable without a token (`GET /api/v1/field/branding` with an ETag; `/api/v1/field/branding/logo` and `/wallpaper` with the image's SHA-256 as ETag and 304s; a `branding` stream message); teams get a display name and a crest per game next to their colour, which stays the team's name for the boxes and the radio (`team_info` on `POST /api/v1/games`, `PUT /api/v1/games/{id}/teams/{team}` until the game ends, `POST /api/v1/crests`, `GET /api/v1/crests/{id}`, `GET /api/v1/games/{id}/teams`), and saved teams keep a club's name, crest and usual colour (`/api/v1/team-presets`); shown in the app (the logo in the header, the wallpaper dimmed behind the check-in's page, the player's view and an Android prop's idle screens but never behind a mode's screen, the accent on the primary buttons made readable at 4.5:1, crests and names in the player's view and on a prop's team buttons; kept per field id; the default look without branding), the GM console's header and scores, the scoreboard and the check-in poster (the logo above the field's name, outside the QR code)
- Uploaded images are PNG, JPEG or WebP only (SVG and GIF refused, the type read from the file itself): logo up to 1 MiB and 16-2048 px a side, wallpaper up to 4 MiB and 320-4096 px, crest up to 512 KiB and 16-1024 px, animated images refused; the controller checks each file's structure and strips its metadata (EXIF, XMP, text chunks, comments) with the standard library alone (`controller/atalaia_controller/images.py`; no image library added, decision BR7), stores it once by its hash and serves it with `nosniff` and a sandboxing CSP; the console reminds the uploader that they are responsible for the image's rights
- Tests: the controller (`test_branding.py`: real and broken images, every endpoint, ETags, persistence, crests, team presets, clean-up), the GM console in Chromium (settings to header, scoreboard and poster, whose QR code still reads back with `zbarimg`; saved teams and a game with crests; the read token), Dart unit, widget and golden tests (`app/test/branding/`, `app/test/goldens/images/branding/`), and the on-device check-in test with the branding over the pinned HTTPS
- The Medic Kit, "Ligaduras" in Portuguese (`modes/medic`, maintainer request): a hit player scans their signed player tag (required) and holds a button for `heal_hold_s` (10 s) to be healed, letting go early cancels; the kit's `charges` (5) shown on its screen and refilled by the GM from the console, `max_heals_per_player` (2) per game at every kit, an optional `cooldown_s` and the `team` it serves; every refusal says why on the screen (forged, another game, not a player tag, wrong team, at the limit, in the cooldown, a hold without a scan, let go early); events `medic.heal` (50) and `medic.empty` (51) with data keys `remaining` (32) and `player_heals_used` (33); its `field.lua` counts each player's heals at every kit on the controller and tells the kits, and a kit out of reach goes on with its own count; screens in Portuguese (default) or English (`lang`); a built-in Field Box preset; tests in the simulator (7 event sequences, in Python and in Dart through the core), two kits sharing a limit through the controller, a kit out of reach, the real controller engine, two host boxes on the host modem, the GM console in Chromium, the Android prop on an emulator (decisions MK1-MK8 and MK9 on)
- Role packages (`kind: role` in `mode.yaml`, modes/README.md): a package that brings a role of its own to any game (`org.atalaia.domination+org.atalaia.medic`); a node given that role runs the package alone, with its parameters only, and the controller makes its join with the package's id alone, so boxes and phones compose as before; the GM console lists role packages apart from the modifiers and offers their roles in the Join tab
- Mode commands: CMD opcode 8 (`mode`, RFC 0001 Amendment 5, proposed) reaches a node's script as `cmd.mode` with a named command; a manifest's `commands` section declares the ones the GM console offers per role (a button in the *Boxes* tab; the controller refuses undeclared names), and `field.send(node, "mode", {name = ...})` lets a field script send its own; the Field Box, the Android prop (radio or Wi-Fi) and the simulator deliver them
- The GM console's log shows events in words where its language files have them (`feed.events`: a medic kit's heals and an empty kit), and an optional team parameter can be left as every team; the app's player view names the medic kit's events in Portuguese and English; the host box takes `@tag` on its console
- Field check-in (ADR-P3-13, `protocol/checkin.md`): a check-in poster per field printed from the GM console (`atalaia:checkin:` + CBOR: field id and name, the controller's https addresses, the field CA's fingerprint, optionally the Wi-Fi name and one game; no secret), with shared vectors `protocol/vectors/checkin.json` checked by Python (`controller/atalaia_controller/checkin_poster.py`) and Dart (`app/lib/protocol/checkin_poster.dart`); the controller's check-in rules and endpoints (`checkin.py`): per-field identities with no account, player tokens keyed with the controller's own per-game token key (decision B8) and their role, team and squad re-checked against the check-in on every request, staff confirmation, the optional federation number (never verified), hand check-ins, per-game replica declarations and the "replicas to chrono" list, the 30-day retention purge, CSV export, `player.checkin` with the player's id only (phase-3-android-players, plan phase E)
- Signed chrono readings uploaded to the controller (`POST /api/v1/chrono/readings`): verified with the marshal's registered Ed25519 key, judged against the field's limit template, marking declared replicas passed or refused, a pass valid for the field's day; `chrono.checkin`, `chrono.spotcheck` and `chrono.violation` events (phase-3-android-players, plan phase E)
- HTTPS with the field's own CA (`controller/atalaia_controller/tls.py`, ADR-P3-9): an ECDSA P-256 CA and a server certificate made on first start, HTTPS on `[api] tls_port` (8443) next to HTTP, `GET /api/v1/controller/ca`, `python -m atalaia_controller ca`; the app pins the CA per field on the first scan of its poster (phase-3-android-players, plan phase E)
- The role-filtered live stream: check-in and chrono-list messages (with names) to staff only and to the player whose row it is, player positions only to those who may see them, a player's connection without radio or controller internals; the snapshot now carries the live game's latest 100 events, so a phone that connects mid-game shows what already happened (phase-3-android-players, plan phase E)
- Player positions (ADR-P3-6): `POST /api/v1/positions` with a player token, kept for the running game only, the visibility table (own, squad, team, everyone for marshals and the GM, nobody for spectators) and timed `visibility.granted` grants enforced on the controller (`positions.py`); in the app, sharing the position from Android's own location (no Google Play services) with the foreground service's location type while sharing (phase-3-android-players, plan phase E)
- Android props on the field's Wi-Fi (`protocol/prop-link.md`, decision PD12): `WS /api/v1/prop` with an HMAC proof of the game key; the prop's HELLO, STATE and events go into the controller's FieldController through the radio's codecs, TIME beacons and commands come back; join payloads for boxes carry the field CA's fingerprint and the controller's addresses in optional keys 13 and 14 (RFC 0001 Amendment 4, proposed), which boxes skip (phase-3-android-players, plan phase E)
- GM console: a *Check-in* tab (open or close, the live check-ins, confirm, card seen, team, squad and role, removal, hand check-in, the replicas to chrono, the rules, the poster's settings) and the printable A4 poster page with its QR code and an optional Wi-Fi code drawn from a password that is never stored; starting a game with replicas not chronographed yet asks first (phase-3-android-players, plan phase E)
- The app: checking in at the field by scanning the poster (the CA pinned per field, a changed one refused), the privacy notice, the player's replicas and the per-game declaration with chrono states, the marshal's replicas-to-chrono list sending signed series (kept and sent later when out of reach), the player's view over the pinned HTTPS with the player token, the prop's Wi-Fi link to the controller (events, STATE, commands, `node.tamper`), and for the Android controller role a minimal MQTT 3.1.1 broker and the position visibility engine; European Portuguese and English (phase-3-android-players, plan phase E)
- Tests: controller (check-in, tokens, the stream by role, the chrono list with the shared vectors, retention, privacy in events, MQTT, logs and read views, positions, the prop link, a real TLS connection pinned to the field CA), console browser tests (the Check-in tab live, the poster's QR code read back with `zbarimg`), Dart unit and widget tests against a fake controller, and an on-device test checking in over real HTTPS (phase-3-android-players, plan phase E)
- `app/README.md`: trying the app on the Android emulator from Windows (Hypervisor Platform, the new `android` SDK tool, a stock API 36 image, the controller in Docker at `10.0.2.2`), hot reload and the on-device tests with Flutter on Windows (phase-3-android-players)
- The app's screens, in European Portuguese (the default) and English: a role selector (the game prop on Android only, the player, the marshal, the GM console), settings (language, theme, the field join key, the admin PIN, kiosk mode) and a battery guide by phone maker (phase-3-android-players, plan phase D)
- The Android prop shell (`app/lib/prop/`): any built-in mode on the shared core in a game loop isolate, the mode's own YAML screens, big team buttons (press, hold every 250 ms, release), an on-screen keypad, NFC players' tags checked with the game key, an I/O pod's events; sounds as tones and vibration; the game's length ends the game (`game.end`); the admin PIN guards start, pause, end and leave; the foreground service with wake and Wi-Fi locks, the screen dimmed after 30 s and off after 5 minutes, kiosk mode (lock task, device owner possible) with a warning when it is left (phase-3-android-players, plan phase D)
- The screen-template renderer in Flutter (`app/lib/ui/template/`): the core's YAML-subset parser and `{expression}` evaluator in Dart, compared with the core through `dart:ffi` on every shipped template, 2,500 mutated templates and thousands of generated expressions; the Field Box's layout on its 320x240 canvas, scaled to the phone; golden images with the data of the firmware's reference images (phase-3-android-players, plan phase D)
- Joining a game with a join payload on the phone: the QR code (camera), a join card (NFC) or the `atalaia:join:` text, checked with the field join key; Dart join codec and CBOR against `protocol/vectors/join.json` (phase-3-android-players, plan phase D)
- The player's view of a game from the field controller's live feed (time, points, objectives, lives, eliminations, the GM's messages, events in words) and the GM console in a web view (phase-3-android-players, plan phase D)
- The marshal's tools: chrono check-in and spot check on a big keypad with PASS, WARN or FAIL and records signed as they are taken, the replica registry by NFC sticker or QR label, players' tags written for a game, erasing a player's readings (phase-3-android-players, plan phase D)
- The app's Kotlin plugins (`app/android/app/src/main/kotlin/org/atalaia/app/`): device (vibration, tones, screen, battery exemption, permissions), kiosk, foreground service, NFC (reading and writing), a QR scanner on CameraX and ZXing (no Google Play services; the APK check refuses any Google Play services or ML Kit class), and the I/O pod over USB (CDC ACM) and Bluetooth LE (GATT); the capability proxy codec in Dart (against `capability_proxy.yaml`) and the pod client (phase-3-android-players, plan phase D)
- CI job `flutter-integration`: the APK on an Android 11 emulator (AOSP image, no Google APIs) runs the app's integration tests (roles, a Bomb prop on the real core, joining, the player's view over a WebSocket); `python tools/docker_app.py apk` now leaves `app/build/atalaia-debug.apk` for installing on a phone, and `gen` and `goldens` regenerate the embedded modes and the golden images (phase-3-android-players, plan phase D)
- The app runs the shared C core through `dart:ffi` (`app/lib/ffi/`): bindings for all of `atalaia/node.h` plus the version, screen templates, player-tag checks and the event codec; the core's callbacks and a controller's field operations as isolate-local native callables (decision PC1); `CoreNode`, the Dart twin of the simulator's `Node` (phase-3-android-players, plan phase C)
- The game loop isolate (`app/lib/core/game_loop_isolate.dart`, ADR-P3-1): each node in its own isolate, the only code that calls the core; a 100 Hz real-time clock (inputs applied on the tick, game time from `game.start`, stopped while paused) or a manual clock for tests and replays; events, commands, log lines and status changes delivered in order; node runners chosen by a conditional import (`node_runner.dart`), with no core on the web and none on iOS (phase-3-android-players, plan phase C)
- The FFI stub (`core/tests/stub.c`, CMake `-DATALAIA_STUB=ON`): `node.h` with fixed behaviour for the bridge's tests, and a report of the C structs' sizes and offsets from the real headers, against which both the Dart structs and the simulator's ctypes structs are checked (`app/test/ffi/layout_test.dart`, `simulator/tests/test_ffi_layout.py`); CMake option `ATALAIA_MBEDTLS` to build without mbedtls (phase-3-android-players, plan phase C)
- Every mode's tests (`modes/*/tests/*.yaml`) now also run on the real core through the Dart bridge, each with the nodes called directly and with each node in its own game loop isolate, with the simulator's checks (event schema, LoRa payload size through the core's codec, screen templates): `app/test/modes/`, a Dart port of the simulator's runner (phase-3-android-players, plan phase C)
- The core for Android: `core/CMakePresets.json` cross-compiles it with NDK 28.2.13676358 and CMake 3.22.1 for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a and x86_64 (64-bit Lua numbers, no warnings, 16 KB pages), and the app's Android project (`app/android/`, `org.atalaia.app`, Android 8.0 and up; Gradle 9.1.0 checksum-pinned, Android Gradle plugin 9.0.1, Kotlin 2.3.20) builds it into the APK through `externalNativeBuild`; a placeholder screen shows the core's version (phase-3-android-players, plan phase C)
- `python tools/docker_app.py` runs the whole app pipeline from any shell: it builds the core and the FFI stub in `atalaia-dev`, then the Dart checks and tests (`check`), the NDK cross-compile (`ndk`) and `flutter build apk --debug` (`apk`) in the pinned Flutter image, each library checked (machine, exported functions, dependencies, page alignment); CI jobs `flutter-test` (replacing "Player app logic (Dart)"), `core-android-ndk` and `flutter-build-android`; `ffi` 2.2.0 pinned (phase-3-android-players, plan phase C)
- The app's first code (`app/`, Dart, no user interface yet): chrono check-in logic (energy `0.5·m·v²` and fps/m/s, `ChronoReading` and `Replica` per ADR-P3-7, check-in PASS/FAIL and spot-check PASS/WARN/FAIL against the class limit and the check-in plus 5%, retention and erasure rules), limit templates in YAML with their loader (Portugal, United Kingdom, Germany, generic CQB: figures from the project's research, marked unverified in every file: check them against current national and association rules before use; decision P3-5), and player tags (`nfc_tag_signer.dart`, byte for byte against `protocol/vectors/tag.json`) (phase-3-android-players, plan phase B)
- Signed chrono records (`protocol/chrono-record.md`): one shot per record, signed with the marshal's Ed25519 key over a precisely defined canonical JSON (the same bytes as Python's `json.dumps(sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False)`); Dart signer and verifier (`app/lib/chrono/record_signer.dart`), Python verifier for the controller and the site server (`controller/atalaia_controller/chrono.py`), shared vectors `protocol/vectors/chrono_record.json` (4 valid, 28 invalid, RFC 8032 known answers) (phase-3-android-players, plan phase B)
- Player tokens (`protocol/player-token.md`): `apt1.` + canonical JSON claims (player, team, squad, role, game, expiry) + HMAC-SHA256, keyed with a controller-only token key (decision B8); Dart (`app/lib/security/player_token.dart`) and Python (`controller/atalaia_controller/player_token.py`, not wired into the API until plan phase E), shared vectors `protocol/vectors/player_token.json` (3 valid, 22 invalid) (phase-3-android-players, plan phase B)
- `python tools/docker_app.py`: the app's `flutter analyze`, `dart format` check and `flutter test` in `ghcr.io/cirruslabs/flutter:3.44.0` (pinned by digest), and CI job "Player app logic (Dart)" running the same `app/tool/check.sh`; dependencies pinned (`crypto` 3.0.7, `cryptography` 2.9.0, `yaml` 3.1.4) with `pubspec.lock` committed (phase-3-android-players, plan phase B)
- Phase 3 events 25-27 and 33-41 (`kill.confirmed`, `kill.denied`, `team.eliminated`, `money.collected`, `money.deposited`, `chrono.checkin`, `chrono.spotcheck`, `visibility.granted`, `visibility.expired`, `node.tamper`, `pod.connected`, `pod.disconnected`) and data fields `see_team` (29) and `tolerance_pct` (30), with three new shared event vectors; a draft RFC records them (`docs/rfcs/0000-phase-3-events-tags-and-pods.md`) (phase-3-android-players, plan phase A)
- Player tags (`protocol/nfc-tag.md`): the player id and team signed with the game key (HMAC-SHA256, 16 bytes) in an `application/vnd.atalaia.tag` NFC record; C (`core/src/tag.c`) and Python (`simulator/atalaia_sim/tag.py`) codecs checked against `protocol/vectors/tag.json`; the host checks a tag and gives the mode `nfc.tag` (phase-3-android-players, plan phase A)
- Three modes built on player tags, built into the Field Box as presets 6-8, with European Portuguese names: Kill Confirmed (`modes/kill_confirmed`: an enemy's tag confirms a kill, a teammate's denies it), Respawn and Lives (`modes/respawn`: lives per team or per player, cooldown, `team.eliminated`) and Player Check-in (`modes/player_checkin`: counts per team, duplicates refused); simulator test step `scan` (phase-3-android-players, plan phase A)
- Capability proxy protocol v1 for the I/O pod (`protocol/capability-proxy.md`): the USB modem's framing, IDENT/EVT/CMD/ACK in CBOR, the modem's radio frames shifted by 3, BLE GATT UUIDs; C payload codec (`core/src/proxy.c`), frames tested with the modem framing (`ctest -R proxy_codec`), Python reference codec, shared vectors in `protocol/vectors/capability_proxy.yaml` (phase-3-android-players, plan phase A)
- Declarative screen templates (ADR-P3-4): YAML screens in `modes/<mode>/assets/screens/`, parsed and evaluated in the core (`core/src/screen_template.c`: a YAML subset, `{expressions}` with `|clock`, `|upper`, `|int`), drawn on the Field Box by an LVGL renderer that patches only what changed and falls back to the C screens; Bomb and Domination migrated (Domination now shows a screen while it runs), 16 new reference images; the simulator checks every template with the core's parser (phase-3-android-players, plan phase A)
- Field Box: player tags read during a game (PN532 every 300 ms, or `@tag` on the serial console), team buttons send `team_input.press`, the display is told the running mode (`@mode`), a Wokwi Kill Confirmed scenario (phase-3-android-players, plan phase A)
- Field radio channel choice (RFC 0001 Amendment 3, decisions CH1-CH13): the field's channel is a controller setting, chosen in the GM console's new *Definições / Settings* tab while no game is live: keep 869.525 MHz (recommended, with the reason shown), "find a quiet channel" (the USB modem listens to each candidate for an equal share of `[radio] scan_dwell_s`, 60 s by default, and the controller ranks them and suggests the quietest 500 mW channel when it is quiet enough, otherwise the quietest of all), a random legal channel (shown as a suggestion with its odds), or one from the list. The choice survives a restart, goes to the modem (SET_CHANNEL, CONFIGURE) and to the boxes in the join payload; the console reminds the GM until it has been chosen once. API: `GET /api/v1/radio`, `PUT /api/v1/radio/channel`, `POST/GET/DELETE /api/v1/radio/scan`, `POST /api/v1/radio/random`; WebSocket `radio` and `radio_scan` messages; `[radio] scan_dwell_s` and `quiet_threshold_dbm` (phase-2-field-network)
- USB modem MEASURE command (CMD 0x08) and NOISE frame (0x08): listens on a channel for 10 ms-10 s without transmitting and reports instantaneous-RSSI statistics (min, mean, median, 90th percentile, max) and LoRa channel-activity detections; refused while a game is configured (ERROR busy) or without a radio (ERROR no_radio); IDENT `measure`, STATUS `measuring` (`protocol/usb-modem.md`, `main/radio/noise_stats.c`, the SX1262 HAL's `measure()`, simulated noise levels in the host modem) (phase-2-field-network)
- Why 869.525 MHz is the default, with the regulatory figures and their sources (ERC Recommendation 70-03 Annex 1, ETSI EN 300 220-2, LoRaWAN Regional Parameters RP002-1.0.4), in RFC 0001 Amendment 3, README.md, controller/README.md, the example config, the GM console and the project page's radio section (phase-2-field-network)
- Two radio diagrams in README.md and the project page ("How the radio works"): how LoRa works (chirps below the noise, spreading factor, range in the open and indoors, relaying with the hop limit, the EU868 rules) and which Skirmesh-RF layers each device runs, built and planned. `tools/build_site.py` builds them from `site/src/diagrams/*.svg` into `site/img/diagrams/` in each language, in a wide and a narrow layout, wraps their text and fails `--check` when a translation does not fit (measured with the widest common fallback font)
- Phase 2 mode pack: Hold-All Domination (`modes/holdall`, the controller's `field.lua` scores a team only while it holds every box), Last Stand (`modes/laststand`, a countdown to defeat while the defenders hold their last zone; the attackers win), per-team keypad codes on Bomb (`team_codes`), and the Codes modifier (`modes/codes`: keypad codes for points, hints and unlocks, reported to the GM with the holding team). New events 28–32 (`holdall.*`, `laststand.*`, ids from ADR-P3-8) and data fields `duration_s` (28) and `holder` (31) (phase-2-field-network, mode pack)
- Modifiers (`kind: modifier`) run alongside a main mode on the same box; a game names them as `main+modifier`, and the simulator, the controller and the Field Box compose the scripts the same way (phase-2-field-network, mode pack)
- Translated mode names and parameter labels (`i18n` in `mode.yaml`, European Portuguese for every built-in mode), shown by the GM console; modifier choice in the console's game and preset forms (phase-2-field-network, mode pack)
- The simulator runs a mode's `field.lua` as the controller does (`keys` test step, `serve --modifier`); the controller keeps `field.lua` points across restarts, restarts the script after a restart or takeover, and ends the game on a script's `game.win()` (phase-2-field-network, mode pack)
- Field Box: `code_entry` screen; all five modes built in as presets (a box adds missing built-in modes at boot and refreshes their scripts); joins with modifiers; Wokwi Codes scenario (phase-2-field-network, mode pack)
- Project page and README gallery: the game picker now lists all five modes, and the Codes modifier's code-entry screen is shown (phase-2-field-network, mode pack)
- Phase 3 (Android and players) design updated for the phase 2 handoff: I/O pod framing adopts USB modem sync+CRC-16; chrono readings signed by Ed25519 per marshal; Hold-All Domination and Last Stand moved to the phase 2 mode pack (event ids 28-32); join payload uses CA fingerprint + Wi-Fi fetch instead of the full certificate; single shared game channel (C5) propagated to pod CONFIGURE; Dart codecs must match schema-exact payloads (phase-3-android-players, commit 3f5ee7a)
- Phase 4 (site server) design updated for the phase 2 and 3 handoffs: custom-scenario compiler becomes a shared library at `controller/atalaia_controller/scenarios/`; events table unique key adds game time and event type; sync auth is asymmetric (API key controller→server, bearer token server→controller); GPS positions come from phones and pods, not the Field Box (E21); join is by QR, not BLE (phase-4-site-server, commit 9ffda1f)
- Screenshot gallery in README.md and the project page: seven captures of the real Field Box firmware (ESP32, LVGL 9.2, 320×240 TFT) running in the Wokwi emulator, walking through a Bomb game and a field join (bomb-1-pin through bomb-6-defused, join-joined)
- Field Box build manual (`hardware/field-box/BUILD.md`): parts and sourcing, enclosure, board revision and antennas, 1S power path with fuse, reverse-polarity diode and 5 V boost, step-by-step wiring with checks, first flash from Windows, troubleshooting; untested until the first build. Wiring diagrams generated with WireViz from `hardware/field-box/wiring/*.yml`, with GPIO numbers taken from Kconfig (`python tools/docker_hw.py render`), checked by `tools/check_wiring.py` in `check.sh` and CI (phase-1-single-box)
- Join payload (`protocol/join.md`, RFC 0001 Amendment 2): version byte, CBOR body, HMAC-SHA256 tag with a per-field join key; NFC (NDEF MIME record) and text/QR (`atalaia:join:` + base64url) forms; C codec with its own SHA-256/HMAC (`core/src/join.c`, RFC 4231 known answers) and Python codec (`simulator/atalaia_sim/join.py`), both checked against `protocol/vectors/join.json` (4 valid, 34 invalid) (phase-2-field-network, Phase E)
- Controller join flow: `POST /api/v1/join` and `POST /api/v1/nodes/{id}/join` make a payload for a box or a standby controller (text, QR-ready, NFC card bytes; GM token, never cached); `POST /api/v1/controller/join` makes a second controller stand by for a game, follow its TIME beacons and take over when promoted; `python -m atalaia_controller join-key` and `[join] key`; store schema 2 (per-game radio identity, field image, node positions) (phase-2-field-network, Phase E)
- GM console (`controller/console/src/`, Preact + HTM PWA, no build step, served at `/console/`): game control, preset builder from the modes' parameter schemas, live feed with alerts, node list, live scores, field view on an uploaded image, join panel with QR codes, controller status and promote; European Portuguese (default) and English; strict CSP; offline shell through a service worker (phase-2-field-network, Phase E)
- Scoreboards: a read-only page (`/scoreboard`, live over the WebSocket with the read token) and `examples/scoreboard/scoreboard.py`, an MQTT subscriber (phase-2-field-network, Phase E)
- Field Box on the radio (decision D18): `main/radio/field_link.c` sends the node's events (ACK, retry in new packets, flash-log replay after a reboot), HELLO and STATE, follows TIME (time sync with step and hold, leader by term, missed CMDs recovered from TIME), handles CMD and ACK, and relays authenticated packets once; `main/box_game.c` joins games (NFC card or `@join` on the serial console), keeps the join in NVS, runs the mode with the join's node id, seed, teams and parameters, ends and forgets the key, and rejoins after a reboot; PN532 NTAG user-memory reads for join cards; an optional field join key on the setup portal (phase-2-field-network, Phase E)
- Host box (`firmware/esp32/test/box_host`): the box's game and radio code on Linux with mbedtls, for end-to-end tests with the controller and the host modem over a shared UDP air (join, play, battery pull, end, relay through another box) (phase-2-field-network, Phase E)
- Tests: core join vectors and HMAC; 9 controller join and standby tests (two C modems on one air), 3 end-to-end field tests, console and scoreboard tests; Playwright browser tests in Docker (`python tools/docker_console.py`, CI job `console`); firmware host tests for the field link, the box game and the serial console; a Wokwi join scenario (phase-2-field-network, Phase E)
- Field controller (`controller/atalaia_controller`, Python, AGPL): games from mode packages and presets with start, pause, resume, end and automatic end at the time limit; the REST API v1 (the 15 endpoints of spec section 11 plus game list and detail, controller status, manual promote and `/healthz`) with read and GM tokens; the `WS /api/v1/stream` live feed; MQTT topics of spec section 11 through Mosquitto (retained score and node topics, GM commands on `cmd/`); SQLite store whose event log ignores replays and flood copies; `field.lua` run in the C core with `field.*` answered by the controller; recovery after a restart when `persist_game_key` is on (phase-2-field-network, Phase D)
- USB radio modem link: framing with a sync byte and CRC-16 (`protocol/usb-modem.md`), a ping every 5 s, reconnect after 3 missed pings, `radio.down`/`radio.up` alerts, the game configuration sent again after every reconnect (phase-2-field-network, Phase D)
- USB radio modem firmware: `sdkconfig.modem` / `python tools/docker_fw.py modem` builds `main/modem/` (USB-Serial-JTAG or UART0 link, AES-CCM, dedupe on source/epoch/seq, a 16-epoch receive window, nonce-reuse guard, task watchdog); the modem logic is plain C, unit-tested on the host and built into a Linux host modem the controller's tests talk to (phase-2-field-network, Phase D)
- Shared CLAIM election (`simulator/atalaia_sim/election.py`): priority score, missed-beacon detection, candidacy, step-down, manual promote; the simulator's `FieldController` now sends through a pluggable transport, ACKs per box and epoch, decodes events and drops replays, and is the radio side of the real controller (phase-2-field-network, Phase D)
- ctypes `field_ops` in the simulator's core bindings; CI jobs `controller` and `esp32-modem-build`; the dev image gains the controller's packages and Mosquitto (phase-2-field-network, Phase D)
- Core 0.2.0: `state.field{}` read-write/read-only field-level state, `field.nodes`, `field.owner`, `field.send`, `field.score`, `field.common_owner` script API functions (phase-2-field-network, Phase C)
- Radio HAL: SX1262 LoRa driver on SPI3_HOST (GPIO 8-14), EU868 SF7/BW125, single shared game channel (default 869.525 MHz, g2 sub-band, 10% duty cycle, 500 mW ERP) per RFC 0001 Amendment 1; FEM gain accounting via `radio_power.h`, duty-cycle tracking, DIO1 ISR, stub for Linux tests (phase-2-field-network, Phase C)
- Radio task: FreeRTOS core 0 priority 3, TX/RX queues with 8-slot depth (phase-2-field-network, Phase C)
- Flash event log: append-only on LittleFS, immediate flush for normal/critical events, telemetry cache with explicit flush, compaction after ACK (phase-2-field-network, Phase C)
- Time sync: slew adjustment (max 5%/tick, step above 500 ms offset), EMA-smoothed offset from TIME beacons (phase-2-field-network, Phase C)
- Radio protocol: Skirmesh-RF packet encode/decode with epoch persistence via NVS, per-source epoch trial on receive (tries stored epoch then epoch+1, never lower), `tx_blocked` on NVS failure or epoch exhaustion, nonce counter = (epoch << 16) | seq (phase-2-field-network, Phase C)
- NVS key storage: game key and epoch in "radio" NVS namespace, function-pointer abstraction for testability (`atalaia_radio_nvs_ops_t`), ESP-IDF backend with optional eFuse HMAC encryption (phase-2-field-network, Phase C)
- Partition table grown: OTA slots 1.5 MB to 2 MB, `nvs_keys` partition added for encrypted NVS (phase-2-field-network, Phase C)
- EU868 TX power calculation: centi-dBm integer arithmetic accounting for FEM gain and antenna gain, clamped to SX1262 range [-9, +22] dBm (phase-2-field-network, Phase C)
- Firmware test suite: 10 radio protocol tests (boot epoch, NVS failure, seq wrap, epoch exhaustion, receiver epoch trial with known/rebooted/lower/unknown sources), 6 TX power calculation tests (phase-2-field-network, Phase C)
- ESP32-S3 firmware shell for the Atalaia Field Box (Heltec WiFi LoRa 32 V4.3): HAL abstraction layer with ESP-IDF and Linux stub implementations for buttons, keypad, LEDs, display, NFC, audio, and battery (phase-1-single-box)
- Game timer with configurable countdown and score freeze: sends `game.end` at expiry, compatible with all existing modes (phase-1-single-box)
- Preset storage on LittleFS: save, list, load, and delete up to 32 presets with associated mode source files (phase-1-single-box)
- State persistence to LittleFS: Lua state table, game time, and scores written every second and resumed after a reboot (phase-1-single-box)
- Keypad-driven preset picker with admin PIN entry, preset selection, timer adjustment, and start confirmation (phase-1-single-box)
- Screen renderer for nine templates (`standby`, `countdown`, `defused`, `exploded`, `final_score`, `paused`, `status`, `preset_select`, `wifi_setup`) backed by LVGL v9 on ESP32 (phase-1-single-box)
- Wi-Fi captive-portal first-time setup: SoftAP, DNS redirect, and embedded HTML configuration page that writes to NVS (phase-1-single-box)
- Memory monitor that logs DRAM and PSRAM heap stats every 10 s on ESP32 (phase-1-single-box)
- HAL stub integration test suite: 32 assertions across 8 test suites (game timer, command dispatch, preset store, state persistence, node runner, preset UI, config store, integration), all runnable on Linux in the existing `atalaia-dev` container (phase-1-single-box)
- ESP-IDF cross-compile CI gate (`esp32-build` job) and firmware stub test CI gate (`firmware-test` job) added to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (phase-1-single-box)
- Dockerfile for ESP-IDF cross-compilation at `tools/dev/Dockerfile.esp32` (phase-1-single-box)
- Hardware bring-up runbook at `firmware/esp32/docs/BRING_UP.md`: first-boot smoke test through the 2-hour stability gate (phase-1-single-box)
- Field Box screens drawn with LVGL 9.2 on the device: 12 templates for the 320x240 TFT (`standby`, `countdown`, `defused`, `exploded`, `final_score`, `paused`, `status`, `wifi_setup` and the preset picker's `pin_entry`, `preset_select`, `timer_adjust`, `start_confirm`), team screens in the team's colour, built once and patched with new data; a display port with two DMA draw buffers (phase-1-single-box)
- Screen reference images: the host tests build the same screen code against LVGL 9.2.2 (fetched from its GitHub tag, checked against `dependencies.lock`) with the device's `lvgl_conf/lv_conf.h`, render every template headless and compare it pixel by pixel with `firmware/esp32/test/screens/*.png`; `ATALAIA_UPDATE_SCREENS=1` updates them (phase-1-single-box)
- Wokwi emulator setup for VS Code: `firmware/esp32/wokwi.toml`, `diagram.json` (ESP32-S3, ILI9341, team buttons, WS2812 ring, keypad), `sdkconfig.wokwi` (GPIO keypad instead of the PCF8574, no audio, setup skipped, keys from the serial console), a Bomb automation scenario and `firmware/esp32/docs/WOKWI.md` (phase-1-single-box)
- `tools/docker_fw.py` builds the firmware, builds the Wokwi variant and runs the firmware tests in Docker from any shell, including `cmd` (phase-1-single-box)
- Built-in Bomb and Domination presets, stored on a box with an empty preset store; keypad debouncing with one press per key stroke (phase-1-single-box)

### Changed
- The Field Box's OLED shows a held zone's points and how long it has been held (found on the bench on 2026-10-23: the `captured` screen showed only the team and ZONE HELD; decisions BN-23 to BN-25, open): the team big, its points on the box at the right of the header (`12 pts`) and below, from the game's clock, how long the team has held the zone (`held for 2:15`, `ocupada 2:15`, `gehalten 2:15`, `已佔領 2:15`), redrawn once a second (the footer only, one or two of the OLED's pages; the TFT is not touched); while another team holds its button, the bar and that team's name, on `neutral` too. From the data Domination and King of the Hill already send; a `captured` screen without `points` or `since` looks as before. No new letter, so the fonts are unchanged.
- The registry's sample community mode, King of the Hill (`registry/examples/org.example.hill/`, now 1.1.0), draws its own screens, as Domination does (found on the bench on 2026-10-23: a Field Box fetched and played it, but only showed its generic "game on" screen): `assets/screens/neutral.yaml` (the free hill and the hold time), `captured.yaml` (the owner's name and colour, its points on the box and since when) and `final_score.yaml`, with a bar that fills while a team holds its button, in Portuguese and English (the box's `lang`; German and Chinese boxes show English); `display` is an optional capability of its role, and the script, commented for authors who copy it, redraws after a power loss. A third test checks the screens change (`tests/screens.yaml`). The package is 13 824 bytes (5 files); the script is 2.9 KB of the 16 KB preset (7.2 KB with Codes) and takes 26 KB of a Reduced box's 64 KB Lua heap (55 KB with Codes); the screens pass the box's load check (each under 4 KiB, parsed by the core) and draw on the host renderer without clipping or overlaps in English, Portuguese and German. A box that kept 1.0.0 needs its fetched modes forgotten (the console's *forget* mark or `@forgetmodes`) to fetch 1.1.0.
- The target addon's M12 port is **3.3 V only**, with no interrupt line (the maintainer's answer to P5J-9, "fix in 3.3 V", after decision M3; built as decisions P5J-15 to P5J-21, open): the board's 3V3 through a resettable fuse (at most 25 mA for the addon), SDA, GND and SCL of the box's 100 kHz I2C bus on A-coded pins 1-4, pin 5 not connected, no 5 V anywhere, cables up to 2 m, no pull-ups in the addon; *Target Windows* lights the box's own ring, and an addon with lights of its own brings its own cell. The box polls the addon every 20 ms (about 3 % of the bus; a hit reaches the script within about 21 ms, against a 50 ms target), so no GPIO is needed and the pin map does not change. A polling driver with a draft report format (flags, sequence, strength, age, CRC-8; ACK, ARM, DISARM) is in the firmware as a stub, not called yet (`firmware/esp32/main/target_addon.c`, 40 host checks against a model of the addon; the Field Box image is unchanged at 0x1bf220). Corrected in the design specification (section 6), ADR-P5-6's carrier PCB table, `hardware/target-addon/BUILD.md` (the port, the 3.3 V budget, the first-build checks, the polling's numbers and exactly what is still undecided: the addon's processor, firmware, I2C address, "identify" answer, sensor and plate, so it stays not buildable) and the build page in pt, en, de and zh-tw.
- The Lua sandbox (decision P5C-15): `setmetatable` refuses a metatable with a `__gc` field, and `xpcall` calls its handler after the protected call has unwound (it can no longer yield across the call); no built-in mode used either.
- The GM console (`controller/console/`) is now GPL-3.0-or-later with the app-store permission (was AGPL-3.0-or-later), so the app can ship it unchanged for the phone controller; the controller's server code stays AGPL-3.0-or-later (the maintainer's decision PE24-8; LICENSING.md, REUSE.toml, the console's file headers, app/APP-STORE-PERMISSION.md)
- `[controller] persist_game_key` is on by default (decision OT-7): a live game survives a controller restart; the game key at rest beside the field join key, already there, adds little. Set it to `false` to keep the old behaviour.
- A zone nobody holds reads "LIVRE" in Portuguese ("LIVRE · Mantém premido / 3 s para capturar a zona"; the maintainer's decision BN-14, BN-22); English stays "NEUTRAL · Hold to capture".
- A Field Box's `@joinkey` and its setup portal's "field join key" follow the adoption rules (decision AD-7): they no longer replace another field's key (`refused (adopted to another field ...)`: use `@adopt <key> <admin PIN>`); the status screen takes `field` and `fingerprint` rows and the PIN screen a `prompt` (decision AD-2)
- The Field Box pin map leaves the Heltec V4's GNSS connector (GPIO 34, 38-42) to the GNSS module that comes with the board, so later GPS-based modes can use it (decisions E19, E21 changed; PIN-1 to PIN-8; in software and the builds, not yet on hardware). **Rewire a board wired to the old map:** button 2 GPIO 39 -> 48, button 3 33 -> 44 (UART0's RX: no console input on UART0 then), button 4 GPIO 34 -> the keypad PCF8574's P7 (`ATALAIA_KP_P7_BUTTON`, read with every keypad scan), LED ring data 48 -> 35 (the board's white LED flickers with it), display MOSI 40 -> 33, SCK 41 -> 47, CS 42 -> 1 (the board's battery-voltage input, as an output), display RESET and backlight to 3V3. A new Kconfig menu *GNSS* holds the module's pins and power; the firmware keeps it off (GPIO 34 high) at boot; no driver yet. `tools/check_wiring.py` now fails on anything wired to the GNSS connector and allows the three shared board pins only for their one use; the wiring diagrams, BUILD.md, BRING_UP.md and the pin tables follow; the Wokwi build keeps its own diagram's pins (`sdkconfig.wokwi`)
- The site server's breached-password list is kept as hashes only (decision S11-4, maintainer's request): `server/atalaia_server/data/breached-passwords.sha256` (9,126 prefixed SHA-256 hashes of the entries of 10+ characters, 302 KB instead of the 835 KB list in clear), built by `tools/breached_hashes.py` from the pinned SecLists list; the policy is unchanged. The list in clear remains in the git history
- Site server login lockout (decisions S11-12, S12-1): 5 failed logins now lock one username at one address for 15 minutes, not the whole address, so one player's typos no longer lock out everyone at a field behind a shared 4G address; a ceiling of 50 failures in 15 minutes per address, whatever the usernames, stops password spraying; the TOTP lockout per account and the other limits are unchanged
- Phase 4 (site server and advanced play) design refreshed after phase 3's software (design only, nothing built): every ADR re-checked against the code, with dated amendments (the controller opens every connection to the site server and the site server keeps none of its tokens; games identified by game id and seed; scores merged per node; chrono readings stored as signed and public keys only; custom scenarios as a `kind: scenario` package over a built-in objectives role package, since boxes run built-in scripts only; positions never as events; ATAK without player positions on multicast); event ids 42-47 and data keys 34-36 planned; the plan re-cut into 22 small software plan phases in the order to build them (modes, core and scenarios first, then the site server) and 4 hardware ones; decisions P4R1-P4R26 (docs/phase-4-site-server/)
- The project page shows the real progress (maintainer request): the roadmap strip and the roadmap page mark phase 0 done, phases 1 and 2 "software done" (their gates wait for the hardware), phase 3 in progress and lit (plan phases A-F done in software; the phone as field controller and iOS still to come; plan phase G's field test waits for real phones and pods), and phases 4 and 5 "designed" (a new dashed tag, "Planeada" in Portuguese: research, architecture and plan written, no code); the hero's status line says phase 1 and 2 software done and phase 3 nearly complete in software; the roadmap's intro no longer says each phase ends before the next begins
- Project page: section intros and notes use the content's full width, like the images, instead of 66 characters (the hero's lead and the footer keep theirs); the app's screenshots heading gets a section heading's space; the roadmap page lists phase 3's work as done in software and still to come
- Project page: the screenshots open in a viewer on the page (a `<dialog>`, its alt text as the caption, previous and next, Esc, a click outside or the close button, focus back on the screenshot), from a small inline script, the page's first; each screenshot is a link to its image, so without JavaScript the link opens it; still no cookies and nothing loaded from other sites
- Project page: four more app captures from the app's golden images: the role picker and a field's branded player view (logo, wallpaper, team crests) in each language, and the Medic Kit's screens (waiting, holding, healed)
- Project page: "Try it today" adds the firmware (written for the Field Box, the USB radio modem and the I/O pod, run in Wokwi and in tests on a computer, not yet on a real board; the build manual for flashing) and the Android app's debug APK (built in Docker, run on emulators, not yet on a real phone)
- The firmware variant is a Kconfig choice (Field Box, USB radio modem, I/O pod) instead of the modem's own switch; `sdkconfig.modem` still selects the modem. The host modem links mbedtls statically when it can, so it also runs in the Flutter image (phase-3-android-players, plan phase F)
- The project page is now a short main page (about 1,800 words in English, from 4,400) plus four subpages in both languages (maintainer request): game modes (`/modos/`, `/en/modes/`), the radio with both diagrams (`/radio/`, `/en/radio/`), the roadmap (`/roadmap/` in both; "Roadmap" stays in English in Portuguese, replacing "Roteiro") and the glossary (`/glossario/`, `/en/glossary/`, keeping every entry's id). The main page keeps a one-paragraph radio section, three mode cards and the roadmap in one line per phase, each linking to its page, and keeps the `#radio`, `#modes` and `#roadmap` anchors that older links use; every glossary link now points at the glossary page. Every page has the shared header (with a Radio link and the current page marked), a language switch to the same page in the other language, its own title, description, canonical, `hreflang` and `og:` tags, and the footer; `site/sitemap.xml` lists every page with its translations. `tools/build_site.py` builds every page of its page table (`PAGES`) from `site/src/<page>.html` and the partials in `site/src/partials/` (`{% include %}`, `{{ page.to.<page> }}` links, also inside texts), and `--check` now also fails on any link, image or `#id` on any page that leads nowhere, a page missing in a language, an unused template or partial, and generated pages that nothing makes any more
- A field script's `field.send` checks a mode command's name; the controller's join for a box carries only the parameters of what that box runs (Medic Kit)
- The Field Box draws accented letters of templates without their accents ("Le a tua etiqueta"), and dashes, quotes and the ellipsis as their ASCII forms, instead of missing glyphs: its Montserrat fonts are ASCII only (Medic Kit)
- Field Box preset scripts may be up to 16 KB (was 8 KB) (phase-2-field-network, mode pack)
- `code.used` carries `amount` and `holder`; `bomb.armed` and `bomb.defused` carry `reason` (phase-2-field-network, mode pack)
- In the simulator log, events at the same game time keep the order they happened in (phase-2-field-network, mode pack)

### Fixed
- A scenario manifest with a date that does not exist (`version: 2026-13-45`) made `POST /api/v1/scenarios/check` fail with a 500 on the Pi; it is a problem in words now (`bad_manifest`, decision PE24-108, open).
- *Search* on the field-map page could send two Nominatim requests less than a second apart when the wall clock stepped between them: the one-a-second throttle is timed with the monotonic clock now; its browser test measures the page's own sending times on the browser's clock instead of Playwright's route handler's, and steps the wall clock to prove it (decision FL-2, open; it had failed now and then under CPU load)
- The phone controller reads the I/O pod's IDENT flag `measure` (key 8): a pod that says it cannot measure makes the channel scan refuse up front with `old_firmware`, as the Pi does for such a modem; the Dart codec refuses a non-bool flag as C and Python do, and its tests read the `pod_relay` vectors (RADIO_FIELD_TX/RX/ACK, the flags) (decision PE24-105, open)
- The scenario validator, on the Pi and the phone: an id, objective id, code, parameter name or player role ending in a line feed is refused (Python's `$` had matched before it; decision PE24-106, open); an emoji written as a surrogate-pair escape (`\ud83d\ude00`, as JSON in ASCII writes it) is read as the emoji instead of making `POST /api/v1/scenarios/check` fail with a 500 on the Pi (and the manifest refused on the phone), and half of a pair is a problem in words (decision PE24-107, open); shared vector cases for both
- The Field Box's OLED shows a progress bar while a team holds its button to take a zone, in Domination and every built-in zone mode (found on the bench on 2026-10-23, after the held-time change; decision BN-26, open): the OLED only drew a bar a mode's screen sent (`progress`), and Domination never sends one, so holding to capture showed nothing (the bar seen before was King of the Hill's or the Bomb's). The box now draws it from its own buttons on `neutral` and `captured`: it fills over the mode's `capture_hold_ms` (or `hold_ms`), the holding team's name beside it, the points staying in the header and the held time coming back on release; never for the team that holds the zone, and a mode's own bar still wins. The footer only is redrawn, at most four times a second; the TFT is unchanged.
- Missing modes sent to boxes: the field controller took the HELLO of a box that could not reach it over HTTPS as a finished fetch (*done*, the invite *joined*) and then refused the box's `node.modes_failed` as "not a fetch it was sent", while the box left the join (found on the bench, 2026-10-23). The box says HELLO first because the event needs the game's link (P5E-8); the controller now ends a fetch on a HELLO only when HTTPS reports say the box keeps every part it was offered, otherwise waits for the event (the fetch fails with its reason, the node is not counted as joined, the start guard stops waiting, the invite and the console's dialog show the failure and offer *Send missing modes* again once the box announces itself), a late report, or the timeout (`report_lost`); `node.modes_failed` is also taken after a fetch that ended in its HELLO (`load`), and a plain join for the node ends an earlier fetch (decisions P5D-14 to P5D-18, P5D-14 for the maintainer; `protocol/mode-packages.md` section 4, "The HELLO and the event"; no firmware change).
- The app's player screen looked up the app's services in `dispose`, which Flutter refuses (a debug assertion) when the player left the screen while sharing their position; it keeps them from `initState` now (found by the out-of-bounds warning's widget tests, decision MF-40)
- The field controller checks the check-in poster's settings with HTTPS off too: a bad `game_id` was kept and then refused every check-in as "for another game", with no error anywhere (decision PE24-58, found by the shared check-in cases; the Pi controller and the app's controller). Positions are listed by player id, as SQLite already gave them (PE24-57)
- The GM console's map position (ATAK) refused a south-west and a north-east corner of the field image as "a mirror image" (found on the bench, 2026-10-22, decision GR-1): the form asked for the top-left and bottom-right corners and took the two points as given. It now takes any two opposite corners (*A corner*, *The opposite corner*) and puts the northern one at the top and the western one on the left; the controller already accepted any opposite pair. Tested in Chromium with the bench's values.
- A restarted field controller numbers its own events (GM score adjustments, game start, pause and end, `field.lua`'s) on from the stored ones instead of from 1: a second GM adjustment at the same game time was dropped from the log as a replay and its points were lost at the next restart (decision PE24-42, found by the shared restart cases; the Pi controller and the app's controller)
- Firmware updates over the air failed on the first real board (Heltec V4.3, 2026-10-22, decisions OT-1 to OT-6): the Wi-Fi driver could not start (`no_wifi`) for lack of internal and DMA-capable RAM, and once it did, the ECDSA check of the controller's certificate failed with `MBEDTLS_ERR_MPI_ALLOC_FAILED`. Wi-Fi and LwIP buffers and every mbedTLS allocation now go to PSRAM, 6 static RX and 8 static TX buffers, no Wi-Fi code in IRAM, allocations above 4 KB in PSRAM; the HTTPS pin check follows ESP-IDF's certificate-bundle pattern (the field CA the server sent must have issued and signed the controller's certificate, checked by its fingerprint and its signature), and logs why a handshake is refused. The whole update then ran from the controller over LoRa: download, check and install in about 11 s, confirmed after the self-test, reported `done`
- A box that had booted or joined more than ~16 games since it was flashed was never heard in a new game, and the controller after ~16 games was no longer heard by the boxes (found on the bench, decisions BN-7 and BN-9; RFC 0001 Amendment 9 proposed; in software, tested with the C host box and modem over 18 games in a row, not yet on the boards): the nonce epoch is now per game key (an HMAC tag, the last 16 keys and 48 older ones retired in one atomic NVS blob, a new game starts at epoch 1, an old game's card is refused; a box updated mid-game goes on from its old counter), a HELLO or TIME is searched 256 epochs up within a budget, so a box back from a crash loop in a game is heard again (BN-8, BN-20, BN-21), and the controller issues a node id once per game and flags two boxes on one (BN-18)
- The radio on the Field Box, the modem and the pod went deaf for a moment around every packet (found on the bench with two Heltec V4.3, 2026-10-22, decisions BN-1 to BN-3): each receive call restarted the SX1262 with a 50 ms window and gave up on a packet after 100 ms, so a long packet (an invite part of 217 bytes is ~330 ms on air) was cut off and a frame sent right after another (an invite's second part, a game's START before its TIME beacon) was lost; a box could sit in STANDBY after the GM pressed Start. The chip now stays in continuous RX and the radio task only collects finished packets; packets with a CRC or header error are dropped; a send leaves RX through standby so a packet received just before cannot pass for TX_DONE; a channel change retunes from standby. The controller sends an invite's parts one at a time, 150 ms apart after each part's time on air
- A Field Box that rebooted during a game crash-looped with a stack overflow in its main task while resuming the saved game (the join, its buffer and the saved state, ~2.3 KB, on the 8 KB stack before the mode's restore): those buffers are now static (BN-4)
- The Field Box's radio never transmitted on a Heltec V4 (found on the bench, 2026-10-21): the SX1262 there is clocked by a 1.8 V TCXO powered from its DIO3, which the firmware never switched on, so the crystal oscillator never started, the chip still answered on its RC clock, and every packet ended in `TX timeout` (decision RF-1). The radio now powers the TCXO (5 ms start-up) before calibrating, clears the start-up error, and refuses the radio at boot if the chip still reports a device error after calibration. A packet that times out now counts its time on air anyway, so retries stay inside the duty cycle (RF-2). Verified on one Heltec V4.3: every packet sent, about 103 ms of budget per 53-byte packet; two V4.3 boards on the bench then exchanged packets at -37 to -40 dBm with no TX failure (RF-6)
- The console's browser test that reads the check-in poster's QR code back failed now and then with a few characters too many: `zbarimg` also runs its 1D decoders and printed a second symbol ("C3$B", most likely a false Code 39 read in the QR code's modules) on its own line; the tests now run only its QR decoder and still compare the whole text exactly (decision FU1-8)
- The site server's `WS /api/v1/sync/stream` could not be served by uvicorn in its image (no WebSocket library was installed; only the in-process tests had used it): `wsproto` is now a pinned dependency (decision S19-1)
- After a reboot a game picked on the box lost its scores (they were saved but never given back) and its mode's state: a Domination owner or an armed Bomb was forgotten
- A game picked on the box got stuck when its timer ran out: the buttons and keys did nothing, the screen stayed on the last phase, no way back to the picker, and the ended game was resumed after every reboot. The box now stops the game, forgets its saved state, shows the final screen when the mode drew none, and the next key brings the picker back (found on the first V4.3 board)
- The firmware build guide said typed console input does not reach a Heltec V4 over USB; it does (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_CONSOLE_USB_INPUT`)
- The site server's public pages and feeds answer `HEAD` like `GET` without the body (they answered 405), with the same `ETag`, `Last-Modified` and `Content-Length`, and `304` on a matching conditional request: calendar clients use it
- `protocol/chrono-record.md` said a marshal's private key was also kept encrypted on the site server for recovery; it never is: it stays on the marshal's phone and only public keys travel (decisions P4R14, S14-12)
- Security (decision M4-7, M5-1): the code a Hacking Terminal reveals (`hacking.solved`'s `code`) and the secrets in a game's parameters (Bomb's `team_codes`, Codes' `codes`, the terminal's `reveal_code`) reached every player connection on the controller's stream, the read token's REST views and MQTT, where the app only hid them; the controller now strips them for everyone but staff (`controller/atalaia_controller/visibility.py`), and the stream always uses the role-aware route, with or without check-in
- Project page claims that were out of date or overstated: "Try it today" said there was no firmware to flash; phase 2 was still shown as the current phase and phases 4 and 5 had no status; the I/O pod and the Android device were drawn as planned (dashed) in the Skirmesh-RF diagram, and its Wi-Fi phones had the player app "in phase 3"; the glossary said the Android prop and the pod "arrive in phase 3", the chrono registry "will" keep readings, and only the Skirmesh-RF codec existed (the whole protocol is built in software); "Modes are scripts" and the firmware entry said new modes reach a box without flashing and firmware updates come from the controller (a new mode needs one flash for now; updates over the air are still to come); "Any hardware" said Arduino-class boards already play (thin nodes are not built); "Built for fields" called built work "part of the design"; LoRa's 0.5-2 km and the ESP32's one to three days on a battery are now marked as estimates; the field controller entry says the Android controller is still to come; the Portuguese phase 3 text used the English mode names
- The app: closing a pod client from a listener of its own (synchronous) events threw "Cannot fire new event" (found by the new tests against the host pod; decision PF13) (phase-3-android-players, plan phase F)
- The app: a prop's manual-clock tick that was still waiting on the core when the screen closed the session threw; `PropSession.advance` now ends quietly once the session is closed (with the coordinator's fix of notifications after dispose, and a test that fails without it) (phase-3-android-players, plan phase E)
- The controller's SQLite file is created with mode 0600 before SQLite opens it, so its WAL and shared-memory files (which hold the tokens too) are private as well (phase-3-android-players, plan phase E)
- App on-device tests: `pageBack()` looked for the English "Back" tooltip and never found the Portuguese app's back button; role cards below the fold on a small screen (CI's emulator) were tapped where they were not; a new `app/tool/emulator_tests.ps1` grants the notification permission Android 13+ asks for, which needed a person to tap it (phase-3-android-players)
- Core: `{x|clock}` on a value beyond a 64-bit integer converted it anyway (undefined behaviour in C: x86 and ARM showed different texts); the seconds are now clamped at 1e15. Found by the app's template tests against the core (phase-3-android-players, plan phase D)
- Core: `field.common_owner()` kept the team name from one `owner()` call and compared it after the next call, reading memory the host may already have freed (the simulator's and the app's bindings keep only the latest answer), so Hold-All's scoring could go wrong; the core now copies it first, `field_ops.h` states how long an answer must stay valid, and a core test catches the old behaviour. Found by the app's mode tests (phase-3-android-players, plan phase C)
- `tools/docker_app.py` crashed on Windows when its output was redirected to a file or a pipe and Flutter printed a character the console's code page lacks (phase-3-android-players, plan phase C)
- Core: a penalty with a reason and no squad (`score.add(team, -n, reason)`) put the `score.delta` data inside itself, so the event could not be encoded and the handler failed with `script.error`; found by Kill Confirmed (phase-3-android-players, plan phase A)
- A channel centre inside an EU868 sub-band was accepted even when its 125 kHz channel spilled over the sub-band's edge (869.40 MHz occupies 869.3375-869.4625 MHz). Now the whole channel must fit in one sub-band, in the controller's config and API, the USB modem (CONFIGURE, SET_CHANNEL, MEASURE), the join payload codecs (C and Python; two valid vectors moved to legal centres, four invalid vectors added) and the radio HAL (phase-2-field-network)
- The Field Box and USB modem firmware used the 869.4-869.65 MHz sub-band's 10% duty cycle and 500 mW power on every channel; the budget and the TX power now follow the channel's sub-band (1% or 0.1% and 25 mW elsewhere), per sub-band over a sliding hour, with the LoRa time on air instead of a rough estimate, and a packet that does not fit in the budget left is not sent. The simulator's radio model follows the same rules and now refuses to transmit over budget (phase-2-field-network)
- The SX1262 was given LoRaWAN's public sync word (0x3444) although the code said "private"; it now uses the private-network value 0x1424 (phase-2-field-network)
- Project page: the English LoRa glossary entry wrote `class=”nowrap”` with curly quotes, so its range figure was not kept on one line (no other curly quotes inside HTML tags in either language)
- `hardware/field-box/README.md`: the BoM had a 2S pack on the board's single-cell battery socket (it would destroy the board); Vext and the board's ADC were described as used by the firmware; button 2 was on GPIO 5, the V4.3 front-end module's line (phase-1-single-box)
- Heltec V4 radio front end: the FEM's power (GPIO 7, VFEM_Ctrl) was held low, which would have left the front-end module unpowered and the radio nearly deaf on real boards; it is now powered (LDO, then the revision's mode pin, then the chip enable) once the SX1262 answers, for the V4.3 (KCT8103L, GPIO 5 low = LNA) or the V4.2 (GC1109, GPIO 46 high = PA) chosen in Kconfig. Button 2 moved from GPIO 5 (the V4.3's FEM control) to GPIO 39; Vext (GPIO 36, active low) is switched on at boot; GPS is not supported in the Field Box pin map (phase-2-field-network, Phase E)
- Field Box: console input (`@join`, `@joinkey`, serial keys) is read from the board's USB-C port (USB-Serial-JTAG) as well as UART0, since the Heltec V4 has no USB-UART bridge; the LED ring's brightness is applied (40% at boot, `ring:set_brightness(percent)`); `python tools/docker_fw.py build` no longer takes a stray `firmware/esp32/sdkconfig` (it could hold Wokwi pin settings) and `clean` removes it (phase-2-field-network, Phase E)
- ESP32 shell: the serial console code used `CONFIG_ATALAIA_SERIAL_KEYS` in a C expression, which does not compile when the option is off (the Field Box default); found by a clean build (phase-2-field-network, Phase E)
- The core's payload codec (`msg_codec.c`) did not follow the schema and RFC 0001: HELLO capabilities, STATE counters, CMD params and CLAIM acks had other CBOR types, required fields were dropped when zero, and nothing was checked on decode; its vector tests skipped every vector it could not read, as did the Python ones. Both codecs now agree on every shared vector, byte for byte, and reject the invalid ones (phase-2-field-network, Phase D)
- The simulator's ctypes structs lacked `field_state` and `field_ops`, so the core read past the end of the Python config; the simulator's CBOR codec could not encode the ACK bitmap (phase-2-field-network, Phase D)
- Radio HAL: a BUSY timeout or SPI error is now returned to the caller instead of ignored, so a missing SX1262 is reported as "not detected" and released; the debug-step logs are debug level (phase-2-field-network, Phase D)
- Firmware: the memory monitor task overflowed its stack and crash-looped the box; the HAL drivers were never initialised; LittleFS was never mounted; the battery gauge and NFC reader claimed two I2C drivers on the same pins; the display flush queued an SPI transaction from the stack; the Wi-Fi setup never initialised Wi-Fi. Missing parts are now logged and skipped at boot (phase-1-single-box)
- `hardware/field-box/README.md` pin table now matches the Kconfig defaults (PCF8574 keypad, I2S on GPIO 6/15/16, backlight on GPIO 47, display RST on GPIO 38) (phase-1-single-box)
